## Approved scope and final implementation plan

### Datasets and scoring

- Expand Brief from 12 to 24 cases: missing information, optional price, importance, categorical/boolean requirements, delivery dates/periods, ambiguity and non-invention.
- Retain ten clarification cases; add optional-field and existing five-question regressions. `clarification.py` already defines `MAX_QUESTIONS=5`, slices unresolved fields and validates the result size. Exercise this behavior only as a regression of the existing runtime. Do not introduce, alter or independently impose a production cap in M6-U1; if that behavior is absent in the implementation base, omit the cap regression and report the difference.
- Add five positive supplier-explanation scenarios (clear match, required failure, Unknown, Conflict, near match); retain adversarial controls.
- Expand quotes to 12 PDFs: ten extractable plus malformed and image-only. Add included tooling, missing MOQ, quantity mismatch, non-USD and explicit unknowns; preserve primary PDFs.
- Reuse comparison/advice and add general/project facts, actual mixed currencies, unequal quantities, required conflicts and stale rejection. Report comparison and suggestion separately from combined advice.
- Separate live scenarios and scorer controls. Compare exact facts/states/numbers/importance/evidence with documented bounded aliases and order-insensitive collections. Label wording heuristics; prepare difficult-case human review.
- Named assertion failures and numerator/denominator metrics; critical hallucination, correctness, uncertainty and provenance findings prominent.

### Runner and safeguards

- Internal command: python -m sourcepilot.ai.eval_baseline --mode offline|live --output <directory>, optional category selection; preserve existing entry points.
- Offline validates fixtures, parses PDFs, scores recorded outputs and negative controls; no provider construction.
- Live uses existing structured-operation and Sourcing Analyst entry points, client injection and real tools over eval-owned records. Synthetic responses only via existing seams preserving validation, scoping, retries and limits. No parallel runtime, tools or agent.
- EVAL_DATABASE_URL is eval-only. Validate provider/database configuration, connectivity, Alembic heads/schema readiness and fixture/scorer validity before any provider call or fixture write. No automatic migration/reset; no application configuration change or startup dependency. Create only fresh eval-owned records; do not mutate existing projects/shared suppliers.
- Reproducibility uses pre-run HEAD, pre-run dirty status and SHA-256 hashes of only files materially consumed by the selected eval: its runtime/adapters/scorers/contracts/domain inputs, selected datasets and expected/recorded outputs, selected PDFs, prompt definitions and dependency locks. Use a small explicit list, scoped to selected categories. Record prompt versions/hashes, dataset hashes, configured model and timestamp. Recheck consumed-file hashes at the end and invalidate provenance if inputs changed. Exclude unrelated repository files, secrets, reports and documentation that the eval does not consume. No generalized repository source-manifest framework and no final/self-referential commit SHA requirement.
- Machine-readable cases and readable summary with partial results, outputs, assertions, run IDs and existing AIRun metadata only. No M6-U2 observability work. Ignore working reports; retain reviewed synthetic baseline evidence without credentials/tokens/headers.

### Completeness and exit semantics

- Completeness is relative to selected categories: every required case in the selected scope must have exactly one terminal, inspectable result. A category-filtered run is complete when that selected scope is accounted for; it must be labeled as filtered and must not be represented as a full baseline. Record selected categories, required case IDs, expected/actual counts, completeness and full-scope flags. Recorded full offline and live baselines require all six categories. Missing, duplicate or unexpectedly skipped cases make the run INCOMPLETE; explicitly expected no-provider paths are successful cases, not omissions.
- Offline mismatch, missed negative rejection or unexpected parser outcome: REGRESSION_FAILED, exit 1.
- Configuration/database/preflight/provider system or transport/harness/adapter/scorer/report-generation failure preventing trustworthy execution: ERROR/INCOMPLETE, exit 2 (takes precedence over exit 1 and quality findings). A contract failure caused by a broken harness, fixture, adapter or schema integration is an execution error; model output rejected correctly by the existing production contract is classified separately below. Unexpected runtime exceptions or ambiguous failure attribution remain execution errors rather than assumed model-quality findings.
- Expected negative-control or unsupported-PDF rejection: EXPECTED_REJECTION, successful check.
- Live factual quality failure or correct production termination after model-caused structured-output repair exhaustion or Analyst turn/tool limits: QUALITY_FINDING. Preserve production AIRun termination status, attempted output and validation/limit reason alongside the eval classification. This accounts for the case without bypassing validation, increasing limits or rerunning until success. A trustworthy complete run with such findings is COMPLETED_WITH_FINDINGS, exit 0, rather than exit 2. Exit 0 means trustworthy execution, not model-quality acceptance.
- Required omitted/unexpectedly skipped cases: INCOMPLETE. Intended no-model cases explicit. Continue independent cases; existing retries only, no hiding failures through reruns.
- Critical findings documented with release implications. Production fixes require separate scope approval.

## Tests and acceptance criteria

- Validate IDs, fixture/output contracts/correspondence, PDF blocks/hashes and expected failures.
- Explicit scorer-sensitivity tests start with a known acceptable output, mutate one material property and assert the named assertion fails: invented fields/capabilities; invented or altered prices; wrong currency; invalid, wrong-scope or misattributed evidence; hidden required failures; lost Unknown or Conflict; incorrect correction provenance (comparison must use the user-corrected value while retaining original extracted evidence); forced suggestions where evidence requires no suggestion; unsupported numeric supplier/match scores. Keep valid controls and bounded aliases so tests demonstrate both rejection and legitimate acceptance.
- Test selection/scoped completeness/full scope, offline provider prohibition, live scoring, partial reports, all status/exit classifications and precedence.
- Failed preflight: zero calls/writes; no existing project changes; production does not require EVAL_DATABASE_URL.
- Verify existing runtime validation/scoping/retries/limits and stale no-call rejection.
- Run affected tests, evaluator/provenance/runtime regressions, API lint/type checks, full offline/live baselines. Offline only in normal CI.
- Accept passing regression/scorer/harness checks, complete categories, metrics and reproducible full-baseline evidence with triage. Human-approved revision (2026-11-03): documented terminal semantic-validation failures may satisfy the live-evidence requirement when every required case is accounted for, failure outputs/traces are inspectable, and critical findings remain explicit unresolved product-quality risks. Preserve RUNTIME_ERROR and ERROR/exit 2 classifications; this does not make failed cases or runs successful. Missing cases, untrustworthy evidence, or configuration/provider/harness/scorer failures preventing required evidence still do not satisfy acceptance. A filtered follow-up remains separate and cannot replace a full baseline or establish complete current-version full-run success.

## Explicitly out of scope

Public API, schema, production cap/prompt/contract/business-rule changes; dependencies/infrastructure; M6-U2 tracing/Langfuse polish; Review, Done Check or commit. Use configured model; human review pending until actually performed.

## Browser QA requirement

Browser QA: Not applicable

Reason: Internal eval tooling with no browser-visible changes.

## Configuration and baseline execution policy

Use the configured `SOURCEPILOT_AI_MODEL`. Live execution requires an already-configured dedicated `EVAL_DATABASE_URL`; never fall back to application/test configuration or create, migrate, reset or repurpose a database to satisfy it. This restriction governs runner/preflight behavior; the separate human-authorized manual provisioning recorded below created and migrated only the new dedicated database, without changing that runtime policy. Preflight must validate configuration, connectivity and migration readiness read-only before any provider call or fixture write. Only after preflight may the existing runtimes operate on fresh eval-owned synthetic records. No mutation of existing projects or shared supplier records, no schema change, and no production startup dependency on eval configuration.

If dedicated eval configuration is absent or readiness fails, record the preflight blocker with exit 2, make zero provider calls and zero fixture writes, and stop live execution. Offline implementation verification can continue; the full live-baseline acceptance criterion remains pending.

## Expected file impact

- `apps/api/evals/`: selected case datasets, recorded offline controls, category documentation and guard/parser fixtures.
- `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/`: eval runner, thin production-runtime adapters and scorers; existing feature eval helpers only where necessary. No production runtime/prompt/contract changes.
- `apps/api/tests/`: runner/preflight/status/completeness/scorer tests, runtime adapter regressions, synthetic PDF fixtures and expected blocks.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.gitignore`: offline-only CI invocation and working-report exclusions if needed.
- `docs/implementation/M6-U1.md`, implementation index and retained synthetic baseline evidence: plan/results, reproducible reports and triage. No protected specification edits.

## Delivery sequence and acceptance

1. Validate and complete the six category datasets, retaining existing primary demo fixtures and separating positive scenarios from negative controls.
2. Specify assertions, metrics and scorer limitations; prove sensitivity with the targeted mutations above.
3. Wire offline execution and thin live adapters to existing entry points and injection seams, preserving validation, scoping, retries and limits.
4. Add read-only eval database preflight, scope-relative completeness, explicit status/exit semantics and simple consumed-input fingerprints.
5. Produce machine-readable and readable reports; include per-case outcomes, named assertions, numerator/denominator metrics, critical findings, actual outputs and available AIRun metadata. Add no tracing, Langfuse integration, new telemetry or observability polish.
6. Run affected regressions, runtime adapter tests, API lint/type checks and a recorded full offline baseline. Normal CI remains offline.
7. Run a recorded full live baseline only with valid dedicated configuration. Triage live findings and mark difficult-case human review as pending until actually performed. Do not tune production behavior in this unit.

Acceptance requires all categories runnable, complete and inspectable full-baseline evidence under the human-approved live-evidence criterion above, passing offline/harness/scorer regressions, documented metrics and limitations, inspectable failures and visible critical correctness/hallucination findings. Expected negative rejections pass. Live quality findings and documented terminal semantic-validation failures retain their release implications and original classifications; filtered runs remain separate and do not replace the full baseline.

The configured `SOURCEPILOT_AI_MODEL` is present in `.env`; the live adapter reads that existing setting rather than selecting another model. No live provider call was made. The live baseline requires an already-configured dedicated eval database at the existing repository migration heads. Its acceptance requirement remains pending.

## Review findings

`IN_REVIEW`. The seven approved independent-review findings and four follow-up findings were addressed on 2026-10-06. The three further prose findings were corrected at the evaluation boundary on 2026-10-07: bounded lexical cues now produce named **heuristic signals**, with no claim of exhaustive narrative validation. Review remains open pending re-review. The missing dedicated `EVAL_DATABASE_URL` remains a full-live-baseline acceptance blocker. Browser QA, Done Check and commit were not started.

| Approved finding | Review fix and regression evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Failure attribution | Classification inspects the captured final model response against its declared schema. Only an independently confirmed schema failure after runtime repair exhaustion, or a recorded Analyst limit, becomes `QUALITY_FINDING`; schema-valid callback failures and ambiguous/tool/harness faults are execution errors (exit 2). An internal `post_validate` `ValueError` through the real structured runtime reproduces `VALIDATION_FAILED` and verifies `RUNTIME_ERROR`/exit 2. |
| Price comparability | `incomparable_price_ranking_signal` flags representative recognized relative claims when currencies or quoted quantities are incomparable. Supplier-bound exact figures and varied warnings are allowed. A non-trigger does **not** certify that other price prose is safe. The difficult-case human set covers mixed-currency and unequal-quantity rankings and warnings. |
| Supplier explanation hallucination | Exact checks retain deterministic states, evidence IDs and application-rendered requirement text. `supplier_free_text_capability_signal` flags representative recognizable unsupported actions or capabilities across all six narrative sections, including claims paired with unrelated valid evidence. Valid warnings and unfamiliar grounded paraphrases are not rejected solely for word choice. The signal cannot prove arbitrary authored facts; human review is pending. |
| Report-write consistency | A failed required report write now returns `ERROR`, exit 2 and incomplete status; the runner overwrites `results.json` with the error state where possible or removes it. Injected failures for both `results.json` and `summary.md` verify no persisted completed/exit-0 machine result remains. |
| General versus quoted lead time | `lead_time_context_signal` flags representative recognizable wrong attributions against the general supplier fact and project quote. Clear 50-day general / 25-day quoted combined sentences remain clear; ambiguous context does not fail from keyword proximity alone and requires human review. The signal is not a general language interpreter. |
| Numeric requirement equivalence | `UNIT_PRICE` requirement amounts compare as decimals, so `6` and `6.00` match while currency and other requirement semantics remain exact. |
| Fingerprints | `pnpm-lock.yaml` was removed from the explicit eval input list. The full offline report fingerprints the API lock and selected consumed sources/data; it does not fingerprint the frontend lock. |

Earlier verification on 2026-10-07: focused narrative-boundary checks **158 passed**; broader runner/scorer/evaluator checks **589 passed**; rollback-based adapter tests **84 passed**; quote-extraction non-reset regressions **3 passed, 1 deselected**. Ruff passed, Pyright reported **0 errors, 0 warnings**, and `git diff --check` passed. The refreshed full offline baseline is **COMPLETED, 90/90, exit 0, 73 PASS and 17 EXPECTED_REJECTION**. Machine and readable reports distinguish exact assertion pass counts, heuristic triggered/inspected counts, and **10 human-review-required examples with status PENDING and no verdict**. `narrative_groundedness` is explicitly `NOT_ESTABLISHED_BY_AUTOMATED_SCORING`; 90/90 means case accounting, not factual proof of free text. Critical triggered heuristics are named in case results and critical findings. Previously accepted failure-attribution, report-write consistency, numeric-equivalence and fingerprint regressions remain passing. Adapter tests used existing normal test database settings loaded from local `.env` with outer rollback; no database was created, reset or substituted. `EVAL_DATABASE_URL` remains absent, so no live baseline was run.

### Remaining P2 correction — explicit ranking rejection — 2026-11-01

The follow-up review reproduced a false critical price-ranking signal for “We cannot conclude that B is cheaper than A because the currencies differ” through both comparison and suggestion scoring. The eval-only price heuristic now recognizes a small set of immediate rejection/uncertainty prefixes: cannot conclude/determine/establish/say, unable to, no basis or insufficient evidence/information to, unclear/uncertain/not clear whether, and do not rank as. These cues govern a named supplier's immediately following recognized ranking only. Each ranking is inspected separately; rejecting one cannot hide another asserted ranking in the same sentence. A non-comparability warning alone still does not suppress an asserted ranking. “B is not cheaper” still triggers because it asserts a comparison rather than withholding a conclusion.

This remains a bounded, non-exhaustive heuristic. It does not establish narrative groundedness or interpret arbitrary negation, uncertainty or prose. The ranking vocabulary, numerical/source checks and production behavior were not expanded.

Verification:

- New focused controls cover rejection/uncertainty wording, asserted rankings with warnings, direct negation, and mixed rejected/asserted claims across conjunctions and sentence boundaries, through both comparison and suggestion scoring for mixed currencies and unequal quantities. Before the correction, all 36 rejection controls reproduced the false positive; the 32 asserted-ranking controls already signaled.
- Focused P2 regression suite: **122 passed**, including the 68 new controls and all 54 previously accepted controls.
- Broader runner/scorer/evaluator/domain/source-resolution regressions: **711 passed**. Earlier failure-attribution, report-write, numeric-equivalence, fingerprint, supplier-grounding, price-alias, lead-context and metric-classification regressions remain passing.
- Quote-extraction non-reset regressions: **3 passed, 1 reset-based integration test deselected**.
- Ruff passed; Pyright reported **0 errors, 0 warnings**; `git diff --check` passed.
- Regenerated retained full offline evidence: **COMPLETED, 90/90, exit 0; 73 PASS and 17 EXPECTED_REJECTION**. Consumed-file fingerprints match current inputs. All **10 human reviews remain PENDING**, with null review results. Clarity and conciseness remain explicitly heuristic, each measured **14/14**, and excluded from exact assertion totals.
- The read-only probe of the already configured isolated PostgreSQL test database failed with `OperationalError`, including a retry with the permitted network access and a bounded connection timeout. Adapter tests were not run; the earlier 84-pass adapter result remains historical evidence only. No fixture writes, database creation, migration, reset, repurposing or substitution occurred.
- `EVAL_DATABASE_URL` remains absent from process, user and machine environments and root `.env`. No live baseline or provider call was attempted; full-live-baseline acceptance remains blocked.

Only the eval heuristic, focused regressions, metric-limit documentation and retained offline evidence were updated for this correction. No production prompts, contracts, schema, business rules, dependencies, tracing/Langfuse or M6-U2 behavior changed. The unit remains `IN_REVIEW`, pending independent re-review. Browser QA, Done Check and commit were not started.

### Eval preflight target-isolation safety correction — 2026-11-01

Confirmed the environment-inspection finding against `preflight_database`: connected database name, migration heads and schema were checked, but neither `DATABASE_URL` nor `TEST_DATABASE_URL` was compared with the eval target. A configured application or isolated-test database could therefore pass readiness and receive eval fixtures.

Added an eval-only isolation guard before engine creation, provider construction or fixture sessions. It compares database name, effective port (default 5432) and overlapping resolved address identities, ignoring credentials and PostgreSQL driver spelling. IPv4-mapped IPv6 and loopback forms are normalized conservatively. Configured protected targets that cannot be parsed/resolved safely, including URL routing overrides, fail closed with a credential-free preflight error. Absent application/test URL settings remain optional and are never used as fallbacks. Existing eval configuration, readiness checks and production startup behavior are preserved. No test-only helper is imported into production modules; no dependency or infrastructure was added.

Regression controls cover separate database names, ports and hosts; collisions with both protected settings; different and encoded credentials; default ports; hostname aliases with overlapping DNS results; IPv6/IPv4 loopback and mapped addresses; and malformed/unresolvable protected settings. Full-runner collision tests assert exit 2/incomplete, no case execution, zero engine creation, zero fixture-session creation, zero fixture writes and zero provider construction/calls. Existing mocked readiness controls now use explicit, separate application/test targets. Application-import controls exercise absent and invalid eval configuration without requiring database configuration or invoking eval preflight.

Verification:

- Targeted preflight/isolation checks: **35 passed, 217 deselected**. New isolation regression file: 24 cases, including 12 full-runner collision controls. All connection/DNS/provider/fixture boundaries in collision checks are mocked.
- Broader runner, approved-review, feature-evaluator, PDF/domain/source-resolution and supplier/comparison evaluator checks: **738 passed, 1 reset-based integration test deselected**. This includes the 24 new isolation/import cases and three existing non-reset quote-extraction checks. PostgreSQL adapter tests were deliberately not invoked.
- API Ruff: passed. API Pyright: **0 errors, 0 warnings**. `git diff --check`: passed.
- Regenerated retained full offline baseline: **COMPLETED, 90/90, exit 0; 73 PASS and 17 EXPECTED_REJECTION**. Human-review and live-quality acceptance remain pending; offline accounting does not establish narrative groundedness.
- An additional TestClient smoke check was stopped after a diagnostic timeout showed Windows event-loop socket-pair initialization stalled before application execution. The final regression uses application import/reload with eval preflight forbidden; no browser or HTTP startup verification is claimed. A test initially assumed SQLAlchemy decoded percent-encoded database names; inspection of the installed dialect showed those names are passed literally. The final equivalent-URL control uses encoded credentials instead.

## Comparison state-assertion binding exception — approved for implementation — 2026-11-02

### Authorization and evidence boundary

Status remains `IN_REVIEW`. Human explicitly approved implementation of this specific production-validator exception in the active goal. Authorization is limited to `_validate_state_assertions`, the two existing test files and this unit record listed below. The earlier production-tuning exclusion remains in force for every other remediation. No protected specification, production prompt, contract, business rule, runtime limit, dependency or infrastructure change is authorized. Live evaluations, independent Review, Done Check and commit are not authorized.

Basis: the existing M6-U1 record, retained `live-2026-11-01-01/results.json` and `triage.json`, relevant production code and existing regression tests, subject to the repository source-of-truth hierarchy. Human clarified that the remediation proposal was supplied in the ChatGPT discussion following live-baseline triage; no separate repository document exists or is required. Planning-only authorization was subsequently superseded by explicit approval to implement the bounded plan below.

### Smallest production change

Change only `_validate_state_assertions` in `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/tradeoff_analysis.py`, including its local matching/binding logic. Combined advice inherits it through `validate_analysis`; no second validator is needed. Retain the existing state vocabulary, outer reference checks, section invariants and runtime. Do not introduce a general NLP parser, fuzzy matching, semantic model calls or a new parsing dependency. A change outside this production function requires further scope approval.

1. **Requirement context across coordinated clauses.** Track a unique, explicitly established requirement within a bounded coordination group, separately from the supplier subject. Permit a continuation such as `Supplier A meets the certification and Supplier B does not meet it` to retain the certification reference while changing owner. Handle a requirement heading followed by a bounded supplier/state list, and labels before or after the state, using the existing requirement-kind labels and exact expected-value disambiguation. Recognize coordinated `and`/`but`, comma-separated supplier/state items, and an explicit requirement heading with semicolon-separated supplier predicates within the same sentence. A new explicit requirement replaces the context; an ambiguous explicit requirement fails instead of falling back to the previous one. Reset carried requirement context at a sentence boundary, a new AnalysisPoint, or unrelated field/topic text. Do not infer arbitrary pronoun antecedents or map parallel `respectively` lists. Every inherited requirement must still be cited in `point.requirement_ids`, and each owner's state must be checked independently. Requirement context must prevent an explicit evaluation-state claim from being accidentally routed into quote-field completeness validation; genuine quote-only claims retain their current checks.

2. **Snapshot-derived supplier aliases.** Build aliases only from unique current snapshot names exactly identifying `Supplier A`, `Supplier B` or `Supplier C`; retain full-name support. Bind uppercase standalone `A`/`B`/`C` and straight/curly possessive forms in bounded supplier-subject positions. Never derive letters from snapshot order, IDs, arbitrary name initials, point ID order, or model declarations. Lowercase indefinite `a` is not an alias. Detect duplicate/case-folded name collisions and alias/full-name collisions without dictionary overwrite or first-match resolution. An alias must resolve uniquely in the snapshot and to a supplier cited by the point. An unresolved explicit supplier subject must not silently use a single cited supplier fallback. Reordering suppliers must not change bindings.

3. **Relational conflict language.** Distinguish the bounded source-comparison construction `quote [field/value] conflicts with [deterministic] evaluation/result` from an assertion that a requirement's state is CONFLICT. Classify only the matched relational span, leaving other explicit state claims in that clause subject to validation. Preserve state assertions such as `requirement is CONFLICT`, `certification has conflicting evidence`, and their existing negative forms. Do not suppress all conflict tokens in a point, trust the output section as a state assertion, or accept arbitrary conflict synonyms. Ambiguous wording remains fail-closed. This distinction removes an erroneous state interpretation; it does not certify the relational narrative as factually grounded.

4. **Keep rejection boundaries.** Keep all supplier/requirement/quote/evidence scope checks, ownership checks, deterministic state comparisons, quote-field completeness checks, required-failure coverage, Unknown/Conflict coverage and section membership invariants. Do not reinterpret a user correction versus extracted evidence as a deterministic CONFLICT. Do not relax stale-snapshot or final freshness/membership checks. Alias resolution applies only to analysis prose binding, never tool arguments or authorization.

### Exact proposed file impact

| File | Proposed change |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/tradeoff_analysis.py` | Only production file: bounded requirement context, collision-safe aliases and relational-span classification. No prompt, contract, runtime-loop or limit changes. |
| `apps/api/tests/test_tradeoff_analysis_eval.py` | Positive/negative binding regressions through `validate_analysis` and the existing `score_case` delegation; comparison retained-response rejection controls. Reuse existing fixtures and parameterization. |
| `apps/api/tests/test_comparison_advice.py` | Pure `validate_advice` regressions proving the combined path inherits binding changes and still rejects downstream section violations; retained `clear-suggestion` rejection control. No suggestion business-rule or prose-validator expansion. |
| `docs/implementation/M6-U1.md` | Record the approved exception, implementation results and remaining failures; retain `IN_REVIEW`. No additional planning document. |

No changes to `comparison_advice.py`, `comparison/advice.py`, eval adapters/runners/scorers, datasets, recorded baseline files, prompt versions, contracts, persistence, frontend, dependencies, infrastructure or source-of-truth specifications. Existing tests outside the edit list may be run for regression verification after implementation approval. If implementation requires another production file or a broader grammar, stop for scope approval.

### Positive and negative regression matrix

| Area | Positive controls | Negative controls |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Coordinated requirement context | A certification MEETS / B DOES_NOT_MEET / C UNKNOWN, each against the correct snapshot state; `and`/`but` continuations; exact requirement heading plus supplier/state clauses; state before/after label; explicit switch from certification to lead time. | Flip the second or third state while retaining a valid first claim; omit its requirement citation; two certifications without exact disambiguation; conflicting label/value; context leaking across sentences, unrelated quote fields or points; multiple suppliers without a resolvable subject. |
| Aliases | Full names and uppercase A/B/C subjects, straight/curly possessives; reordered snapshot and point-ID arrays; same assertion with full name and alias has the same outcome. | Duplicate/case-folded names, alias/full-name collisions, missing letter, foreign supplier, renamed supplier without a defined alias, lowercase article `a`, alias supplied only in model prose, named owner outside point citations, alias in a tool argument. Tool arguments retain UUID-only behavior. |
| Conflict distinction | Source-comparison `quote conflicts with evaluation` plus a separately correct explicit MEETS claim; genuine requirement CONFLICT; existing positive/negative Unknown and Conflict wording. | Wrong explicit CONFLICT/MEETS state adjacent to relational wording; `no conflict` against a CONFLICT state; a relational phrase used to hide a second false assertion; ambiguous relationship; falsely asserting a known quote field is conflicting. |
| Unchanged hard guards | Known-good analysis and combined advice; genuine missing/conflicting fields; valid required-failure and uncertainty coverage. | Foreign IDs, wrong evidence/quote owner, stale/changed snapshot or shortlist, omitted required failure/Unknown/Conflict, known quote in `important_conflicts`, MEETS requirement in that section, invented score, incorrect correction provenance in existing evaluator controls. |

Use table-driven cases with paired mutations, exercising all four analysis sections where a legitimate section control can be constructed. Keep binding-level tests separate from full validator tests: a valid local statement must not falsely imply that the enclosing response is valid. Combined advice still runs its existing suggestion checks only after analysis validation succeeds. Reuse existing freshness/session/limits tests rather than creating a new runtime or duplicate authorization mechanism.

### Expected impact on the four affected live cases

These are predictions from static inspection, not results of a modified validator or a new live evaluation. Historical case classifications and AIRun statuses remain unchanged.

| Retained case | Binding improvement sought | Full-response expectation under unchanged rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| comparison / `unknown-and-conflict` | Preserve preferred lead-time requirement context across the C/A/B predicates; avoid mistaking evaluation CONFLICT for quote-field conflict. | Remains rejected: an `important_conflicts` point cites both A and B although B's lead-time requirement is MEETS, and cites their known lead-time fields. Another conflict point cites the known corrected unit-price field. Additional errors may precede these. |
| comparison / `incomplete-evidence` | Resolve bare A/B/C and preserve requirement context in coordinated state descriptions; retain independent checks for each supplier. | Remains rejected: conflict points cite the known corrected unit price and an A/B lead-time group containing B's MEETS requirement. Independently verify every retained quote ID against the snapshot in later deterministic regression preparation; repeated use of an ID in the response does not establish ownership. |
| comparison / `mixed-currencies` | Resolve A/B/C state lists against unique named snapshot suppliers without guessing from order. | Remains rejected: conflict points cite known unit-price and lead-time fields. Currency warnings and incomparable-price checks are unchanged. |
| suggestion / `clear-suggestion` | Resolve aliases in analysis; separate B's quote/evaluation relationship from an explicit CONFLICT state assertion. | Remains rejected: B's MEETS requirement is included in `important_conflicts`, and conflict points cite known lead-time fields. No claim that the suggestion itself passes, because the analysis gate must pass first. |

Use each retained final response unchanged in regression coverage, together with its verified deterministic snapshot inputs. Reuse the retained results rather than duplicating full responses or creating another fixture/document set. Assert whole-response rejection without depending on the first error message, and add targeted controls for known downstream blockers. For positive binding tests, use separately labeled minimal valid analyses; never repair or silently replace the retained live response to manufacture a passing live case. Other unresolved wording, negation or reference problems stay visible and outside this exception if they require broader parsing.

### Conflicts and unsupported assumptions

- The existing approved unit scope excludes production tuning. This proposed exception amends that implementation-unit boundary only after explicit human implementation approval; it does not change the protected specifications. No conflict with their deterministic-state, evidence, scope or bounded-agent requirements was identified for this narrow correction.
- Retained final model text is evidence of what was said, not proof of supplier names, ID ownership or snapshot states. The existing generic `fixture_snapshot()` is not automatically an exact substitute for a live case's snapshot. Before claiming a faithful full-response replay, verify the corresponding persisted snapshot via read-only inspection or an already retained authoritative input. Do not reconstruct ground truth from the rejected response. If the needed inputs are unavailable, report exact replay as pending; reduced synthetic tests must be labeled as such.
- A/B/C support is conditional on a unique explicit snapshot-name mapping. It is not permission to label arbitrary suppliers by position, nor a promise to resolve every abbreviation in the live outputs.
- The current helper's clause splitting is bounded lexical logic. Support only the coordination forms above needed by focused evidence-derived controls; do not expand into general pronoun, negation, imperative, source-relation or parallel-list interpretation to make retained outputs pass.
- `_validate_state_assertions` does not own runtime freshness enforcement, section membership or suggestion-only prose validation. Leave those boundaries unchanged. In particular, removing a relational false positive cannot legalize a MEETS requirement or known quote field in `important_conflicts`.

### Scope and security risks

- Context leakage could validate a claim against the wrong requirement. Bound context lifetime, prefer explicit local references, reject ambiguity, and mutate later coordinated claims in tests.
- Alias collisions or fallback could attach foreign prose to an authorized supplier. Require a unique snapshot mapping and cited owner, preserve hard ID/ownership checks, and verify reorder invariance. No authorization derives from text.
- Overbroad relational handling could hide real state contradictions. Exclude only a recognized relational span; continue checking every explicit state assertion, including adjacent assertions and negations.
- Production acceptance changes affect both tradeoff analysis and combined advice because they share the helper. Verify both entry points. Do not extend requirement-state parsing to suggestion-only prose or supplier explanation.
- A first-error improvement can mask unresolved invalid references, section invariants or provenance errors in informal reporting. Preserve full-response rejection controls and report binding outcomes separately from whole-response validity and historical baseline classifications.

### Acceptance criteria and later verification boundary

1. Only the exact files listed above change; the sole production change is bounded assertion binding in `tradeoff_analysis.py`.
2. Positive controls pass and every paired ambiguous, contradictory or foreign-reference mutation is rejected. Each coordinated owner/state is checked independently; alias mapping is snapshot-derived and order-independent.
3. Relational language alone does not invent a requirement CONFLICT assertion; explicit wrong states remain rejected. Existing Unknown/Conflict negation and quote-only completeness regressions pass.
4. All four unchanged retained responses remain rejected for unresolved hard-reference/section violations. Local binding improvements are demonstrated independently; no whole-response, live-success or release-quality claim is inferred.
5. ### Remaining wrapped-owner and reset-fallback findings — implementation verification — 2026-11-02

The latest independent Review returned **FAIL**: P1 wrapped references such as `(B)` and `(D)` could retain the preceding supplier, and P2 an unrelated assertion after a context reset could fall back to the sole cited certification. Those findings supersede the earlier implementation-only claims that the narrower controls established complete protection. Human explicitly authorized fixing these two remaining findings within the existing four-file exception. This entry records implementation verification only; separate independent Review remains pending. M6-U1 stays `IN_REVIEW`.

#### Bounded binding corrections

- **P1:** Reference candidate detection is independent of the following predicate and independent of successful ownership resolution. Each explicit candidate must resolve uniquely from the snapshot or reject; it cannot be treated as an omitted subject. The same normalization/resolution path handles full names, bare aliases and supported parenthesized aliases, including possessives and coordinated subjects. Unknown `(D)`, unsupported/unbalanced delimiters, renamed/ambiguous aliases and uncited owners reject. An unrecognized actor before a state predicate cannot borrow an earlier owner. Sentence boundaries also clear carried ownership. Full snapshot names containing parentheses remain supported.
- **P2:** Target classification precedes binding. Unsupported topic words reject whether requirement context is present or has already been cleared. Bare predicates and pronouns bind only through directly coordinated requirement context; the unconditional `labels or point.requirement_ids` fallback is removed. A local generic requirement/result reference still requires an unambiguous citation and cannot contain an unsupported topic. Explicit local requirements can re-establish binding after an intermediate clause; field/topic/sentence resets cannot revive an inherited requirement. Generic quote-field wording remains restricted to the quote-only path and cannot become certification ellipsis.
- The implementation uses the existing bounded lexical vocabulary and snapshot mappings, with no insurance-specific exception, general NLP parser, new dependency or external lookup. Only `_validate_state_assertions` changes in production. Outer ownership/evidence/scope guards, deterministic-state comparisons, section invariants, freshness/runtime enforcement, prompts, contracts, tools and limits are unchanged.

### Assertion-local binding remediation — implementation verification — 2026-11-02

Human explicitly approved the assertion-local remediation plan from the planning discussion, including **independent inheritance of supplier and requirement context**. Implementation and verification are confined to the existing four-file exception. M6-U1 remains **`IN_REVIEW`**. The previous independent Review remains FAIL as historical evidence; this entry is implementation verification, not an independent Review pass or full unit acceptance.

#### Implemented binding rules

Replaced the span-wide binding loop inside `_validate_state_assertions` with bounded left-to-right local units. Commas, supported conjunctions and semicolons separate predicates while exact snapshot names, supported supplier lists and shared field/requirement lists remain protected. Decimal and thousands punctuation does not split numeric prose. Every state-bearing unit must contain one recognized predicate after the existing relational-conflict distinction; multiple unseparated predicates reject rather than share a guessed binding.

- Supplier resolution consumes only a leading grammatical subject/list. Collision-preserving snapshot mappings and the existing A/B/C alias rules remain. An explicit recognized subject replaces ownership; unknown subject text stays unconsumed and rejects before inheritance. Unknown wrapped or multiword names cannot disappear as predicate-less list fragments.
- Requirement resolution checks the complete local target on both sides of the predicate. A recognized label no longer disables residual-topic checks. Unsupported kinds and qualifications remain unconsumed; repeated kinds require exact schema-value disambiguation. A distinct quote field such as sample lead time cannot identify a production-lead-time requirement. Mixed incompatible targets and uncoordinated field lists reject.
- Supplier and requirement contexts inherit independently only within supported coordination. A new explicit supplier may retain an omitted coordinated target, and a new explicit requirement may retain its coordinated owner. Only genuinely omitted slots may inherit; explicit unresolved slots fail first. Bare predicates cannot revive a sole cited requirement after a reset. Explicit generic requirement/result/comparison references retain their existing unambiguous-citation path; impersonal single-owner assertions retain their existing bounded behavior.
- Unrelated clauses clear both contexts; sentences and AnalysisPoints do likewise. Only complete, supported requirement headings seed target context. Predicate-less nominal/list fragments are not assumed to be unrelated clauses: before a following state they require supported non-state predicate scaffolding or an explicit numeric field fact, otherwise they reject. The existing discourse/field reset controls and fully explicit rebinding remain accepted. Unfamiliar ambiguous prose may reject; this bounded grammar does not certify arbitrary narrative groundedness.
- The quote-versus-evaluation relation removes only its relational occurrence and scaffolding; any embedded or adjacent explicit state remains subject to local validation. Residual subjects/topics around a relation must also be consumed. Quote completeness, cited-owner membership and per-owner deterministic state checks remain in force.

The old `named`-wide supplier exemption, `labels`-wide topic exemption, first-predicate-only prefix guard and recognized-subject-dependent assertion boundaries are removed. No production definition or constant outside `_validate_state_assertions` changes. Outer citation/evidence/ownership checks, required-failure and Unknown/Conflict disclosure, section invariants, suggestion validation, final freshness/membership checks, prompts, contracts, tools, retry/turn limits and business rules remain unchanged. No parser dependency, framework, AI judge or other production remediation was introduced.

#### Test-first and regression evidence

The initial new table-driven matrix was added before production edits. Running it against the original worktree validator produced **74 failed, 246 passed**. Both exact P1 supplier examples and P2 insurance example failed their expected-rejection tests through both `validate_analysis` and `validate_advice`, with one/two requirement citations. Valid comma-separated requirement switching also failed, demonstrating the span aggregation defect. The pre-edit production contents are retained in the ignored local preservation record.

Supplementary controls were added before each corresponding correction for distinct contained field names, unknown shared-list members, relation-adjacent unresolved actors, uncoordinated field lists and lowercase/multiword unresolved list prefixes. Those controls demonstrated additional failing rejections during implementation; they were corrected within the same binding boundary rather than by adding supplier/topic-specific patterns. One new uncited-owner mutation initially removed only an owner mentioned in non-state price prose; it was corrected to remove a state assertion's owner, preserving the established non-state behavior.

Final added coverage is **742 passing cases** through the actual validator paths. It includes plain/wrapped/possessive/colon/equal forms; A/B/C and unknown D1/Acme/UUID references; independent snapshot and citation-order permutations; later wrong states; requirement switches and exact same-kind values; unsupported topics before/after/between valid predicates; missing/ambiguous/foreign references; shared suppliers/fields; quote/requirement isolation; relational conflicts; resets and explicit rebinding; all four analysis sections with paired mutations. These are synthetic regressions, not retained-response replay. An additional **164 adversarial validator invocations** over unsupported actor/topic combinations produced zero unexpected acceptances after correction.

### Shared-predicate requirement collection — implementation verification — 2026-11-02

Human explicitly authorized fixing the single independent-review P1 finding: a predicate-less requirement was treated as a heading and silently replaced by a later target. This correction stays within the existing four-file exception. Status remains **`IN_REVIEW`**. The preceding independent **FAIL** and historical implementation/review entries are preserved; this is implementation verification, not a new independent Review verdict.

#### Correction

`_validate_state_assertions` now keeps a separate collection of unresolved explicit nominal targets. Each fragment must resolve uniquely and have its citation before entering that collection. Supported list coordination may add another independently resolved target; a shared predicate validates every collected requirement against every bound supplier. A new heading, unrelated clause, sentence boundary or end of text cannot silently dispose of unresolved targets. Unsupported or ambiguous constructions reject rather than guessing.

Supplier ownership cannot switch while targets await their shared predicate. Standalone heading forms still accept an explicit following supplier/state, and headings separated from prior prose do not revive stale supplier ownership. Successfully validated coordinated targets can provide subsequent local requirement context; generic requirement prose with multiple citations still rejects as ambiguous. Existing supplier lists, independently stated requirements, section/citation/ownership/state/quote guards and runtime freshness enforcement remain covered. No supplier/topic vocabulary, parser dependency, prompt, contract, tool, architecture or other production remediation was added.

#### Test-first evidence and available verification

The initial 180-case table was added before production edits: **36 failed, 144 passed** against the starting validator. It reproduced the first-only/second-only wrong-state bypass and silent heading replacement/reset/dangling-target acceptance. Supplementary controls cover supplier ownership, normal headings, independent assertions, generic-citation ambiguity and inherited shared targets. A further test-first stale-owner heading control failed through both entry points before preserving the heading ownership reset.

Final new coverage comprises **228 cases** through `validate_analysis` and `validate_advice`: every pair of MEETS/UNKNOWN/DOES_NOT_MEET/CONFLICT, both target orders, citation permutations, snapshot ordering, missing citations/snapshot requirements/deterministic results, ambiguous names, duplicate values, foreign values, unsupported targets, ownership changes and context replacement/reset attempts. The all-MEETS control passes; adding a valid target before or after an invalid target cannot conceal the invalid state. These are synthetic regressions, not historical replay.

## Quote Extractor numeric normalization — proposed exception — 2026-11-02

### Authority, status and authorization

**PLANNING ONLY; implementation approval pending. M6-U1 remains `IN_REVIEW`.** Human confirmed that the comparison-validator remediation passed independent review. It is not reopened by this plan. Earlier production-tuning exclusions remain effective except for already approved work; this proposal would authorize only the file-level exception below after explicit approval. No production code, test, fixture, historical evidence or human verdict was changed during this planning task. Done Check and commit are not authorized.

Basis, in authority order: Product Specification v0.2 sections 23–31 (unknowns, source review, conflicts, original currency and human confirmation); Technical Architecture v0.2 sections 13.5–13.7 and 14.6–14.12 (stable blocks, provenance, untrusted documents, candidate extraction and confirmation); Implementation Plan v0.2 M3-U6, M6-U1 and section 12.3; this unit record; current extractor/domain/processing code and tests. No protected specification change is proposed. This is an implementation-unit scope exception, not a new business rule or architecture revision.

### Diagnosis from the retained run

Read-only inspection used the existing `live-2026-11-01-01/results.json` and `triage.json`, both captured responses for every extractable quote, current `quote_extractor.py`, `quotes/domain.py`, `documents/processing.py`, structured runtime/client, active quote cases/curated outputs, parsed-block fixtures and extractor/domain/eval tests. No provider or database connection was used. Independently checking each captured candidate against `validate_manual_values` exposes multiple violations per response; it does not repair, reclassify or rerun that response.

All **10/10 extractable cases** ended `VALIDATION_FAILED`, with two recorded attempts each; both unsupported-PDF cases remain expected rejections. The shared `StrictInt | StrictStr` scalar schema permits strings structurally, but field-specific domain checks reject commercial text in numeric fields. Neither the current prompt nor field declarations explain these numeric representations. The same rules apply to conflict alternatives. A repair often changes only unit price while other numeric violations remain.

| Case | Invalid fields still present in the final captured response |
| --- | --- |
| `harbor-primary` | quoted quantity, MOQ, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `willow-primary` | sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `moss-primary` | MOQ, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `missing-shipping` | sample cost, tooling cost |
| `conflicting-lead-time` | unit price, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `included-tooling` | quoted quantity, MOQ, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `missing-moq` | sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `quantity-mismatch` | quoted quantity, MOQ, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `non-usd` | quoted quantity, MOQ, sample cost, tooling cost, shipping cost |
| `explicit-unknown` | tooling cost |

Examples include `"2,000 units"`, `"USD 40"`, `"AUD 150"`, `"USD 320 to Manila"` and `"Included in unit price; no separate charge"`. Packaging cost is absent in this corpus, so it needs synthetic coverage rather than an inferred live success. Recorded lead-time conflict and explicit-unknown structures appear in rejected responses; that does not establish successful extraction or provenance.

Historical first-error reporting is inconsistent: the narrative table above says eight shipping, one sample and one tooling rejection, while retained `triage.json` lists five MOQ, four sample and one tooling rejection. Domain iteration uses a frozenset and stops on the first failure, so a first error is not a stable or exhaustive diagnosis. These artifacts are preserved as recorded; this plan relies on all captured field values, not a chosen first-error tally. No production error-order change is proposed.

### Proposed extraction instructions and descriptions

Only model-facing guidance changes. Preserve `QuoteExtraction`/`QuoteFields`, all 21 keys, requiredness, scalar union/types, nesting, nullability, state vocabulary and validators. Add Pydantic `Field(description=...)` metadata without defaults or constraints. No response parser, normalization helper, post-response coercion or use of the domain validator's normalized return value is introduced.

| Fields | Instruction and field-description content |
| --- | --- |
| `unit_price` | When KNOWN, a decimal-only JSON **string**, e.g. `"4.60"`, strictly greater than zero, finite, at most four fractional places. No currency code/symbol, separators, units, destination or commercial prose. |
| `sample_cost`, `tooling_cost`, `packaging_cost`, `shipping_cost` | When KNOWN, the same decimal-only string representation, finite and **nonnegative**, at most four fractional places. Explicit numeric zero is valid for these costs; included/free/no-separate-charge prose alone is not an explicit numeric zero. |
| `quoted_quantity`, `moq` | When KNOWN, a positive JSON **integer**, e.g. `2000`, not `"2000"`, `"2,000 units"`, a float, boolean, zero or negative value. Extract the quantity actually quoted, not the requested project quantity. |
| `currency` | Separate sourced three-letter currency code, emitted uppercase, e.g. `USD` or `AUD`. Preserve original currency; do not default to USD, perform FX conversion or infer a code from an ambiguous symbol alone. Missing currency is UNKNOWN even when an amount is stated. |
| Shared `extracted_value` and `ConflictAlternative.value` | Representation depends on the enclosing quote field. Every KNOWN value and every conflict alternative must meet that field's domain rules. Link descriptions to the explicit money/integer/currency guidance above; do not suggest that all fields are numeric. |
| Shared state/source/uncertainty guidance | UNKNOWN has null primary value and no alternatives; CONFLICT has null primary value, at least two distinct valid alternatives, each with its own sources, a nonblank explanation, and the union of alternative block IDs on the field. Preserve meaningful commercial qualifications in uncertainty and their original source blocks. |

The revised `INSTRUCTIONS` will enumerate all five money and both integer keys, their bounds, precision and examples. Add explicit examples: sourced `USD 40` yields `sample_cost="40"` and sourced `currency="USD"`; sourced `AUD 150` yields `tooling_cost="150"` and `currency="AUD"`; sourced `2,000 units` yields integer `2000`. These are instructions to the model to represent supported facts within the existing contract. The application must continue rejecting malformed model responses, without silently stripping currencies or repairing types. Keep the original PDF/block text unchanged and cite the amount and currency sources separately as appropriate.

Retain untrusted-document, zero-tool, no-invention, no-calculated-total and no-FX instructions. Keep timeline fields (`sample_lead_time`, `production_lead_time`, `shipping_time`, `total_lead_time`) as text, including ranges/conditions; the remaining terms/compliance/notes fields also remain text. Their existing nonempty-text/10,000-character rules remain unchanged. The domain's acceptance of some additional Decimal spellings is not tightened: plain decimal notation is generation guidance, not a new regex validator. Do not round an over-precision amount to manufacture a valid candidate.

**Missing and nonnumeric commercial information:** absent values use UNKNOWN/null, no alternatives and no invented sources. Explicitly unstated/TBC values retain the relevant block IDs and explanation. For included tooling, use UNKNOWN/null with `p1-b7` and a nonblank explanation preserving “included in unit price; no separate charge” and that no separate numeric amount is stated. Do not emit the phrase as a numeric value or infer `"0"`. This fits the existing curated included-tooling fixture; no new state or field is needed. Other unrepresentable/ambiguous numeric information likewise remains visible through UNKNOWN/null, sourced uncertainty and unchanged blocks rather than an invented amount. KNOWN amounts may retain conditions such as “to Manila” in uncertainty without contaminating the number.

**Conflicts and provenance:** normalize only a clearly supported representation, never resolve disagreement. Sourced USD 40 versus USD 45 produces monetary alternatives `"40"`/`"45"`, each with its actual block IDs; quantities use integer alternatives. Preserve currency disagreements in the currency field and explanations, without converting amounts or choosing a winner. Textual lead-time conflicts retain their existing text alternatives. Every source ID must belong to the supplied document; missing, fabricated, swapped or misattributed sources must not become acceptable merely because a numeric value validates. Existing validation checks ID membership, not semantic entailment; fixture checks and later live inspection are still required. Source blocks remain the verbatim evidence; the scalar candidate is not a new verbatim-source field.

Extraction remains a candidate draft: processing may copy a KNOWN candidate into extracted/current values with `AI_EXTRACTED` origin and `NEEDS_REVIEW`. Only the user confirms it. Corrections retain the original extracted value, evidence and origin distinction, and only confirmed current values enter comparison. No persistence, confirmation or comparison code changes are proposed.

### Exact minimal file-level exception

| File | Proposed edit after approval |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/quote_extractor.py` | Sole production file. Revise `INSTRUCTIONS`, bump `PROMPT_VERSION` from `quote-extractor-v1` to `quote-extractor-v2`, import `Field`, and add descriptions to the seven numeric fields, currency and relevant shared value/state/source/uncertainty/alternative properties. Keep executable validation/runtime logic unchanged. |
| `apps/api/tests/test_quote_extractor.py` | Add provider-free parameterized contract/schema/source regression matrix below; update the existing traced-success expected prompt version. Verify metadata-only schema changes by comparing schema structure with descriptions removed, including required keys/types/unions. |
| `apps/api/tests/test_quote_extraction_eval.py` | Add provider-free coverage over all ten curated outputs with their actual expected document blocks and existing scoring, plus targeted sourced unknown/included-tooling/monetary and integer conflict controls and paired provenance mutations. Preserve the existing test that rejects a changed request hash. |
| `apps/api/evals/quote_extraction/cases.json` | Recompute **only the ten active `request_hash` values** from final v2 instructions and unchanged parsed-block input serialization. These are curated regression bindings, not live recordings. Do not change expected facts, source IDs, cases, PDFs or `model_outputs.json`. Document the hash rebinding here; never rewrite hashes in retained evidence. |
| `docs/implementation/M6-U1.md` | This proposal/approval and later separately authorized verification results, limitations and preservation record. Keep `IN_REVIEW`. |

No other production or test file is needed. Existing suites outside the edit list may be selected safely for verification. In particular, leave domain validation, `structured.py`, client, processing/service/persistence, eval runner/adapters/scorers, comparison validator, frontend, schema, dependencies, runtime limits and infrastructure untouched. If another edit is necessary, report it for scope approval rather than extending this exception.

### Regression matrix and verification plan after approval

1. **All numeric fields:** parameterize over all five money keys and both integer keys, for KNOWN candidates and CONFLICT alternatives. Accept positive finite money strings through four decimal places, positive integer quantities/MOQs, and explicit zero only for non-unit-price costs. Reject currency-prefixed/symbol-bearing/comma/unit/prose money values, JSON numbers for money, negatives, NaN/Infinity, over-four-place values and zero unit price; reject integer strings, units/thousands prose, floats, booleans, nonpositive integers. Preserve exact submitted candidate strings/types and fail on invalid input rather than coercing it. Do not assert rejection of Decimal spellings the authoritative domain currently accepts.
2. **Currency and unknowns:** USD and AUD with identical numeric magnitudes and sourced separate currency; synthetic additional three-letter non-USD code; no FX or USD substitution in expected payloads. Distinguish omitted MOQ/shipping from explicit TBC and included tooling; all seven numeric keys support UNKNOWN/null. Reject UNKNOWN with a candidate or alternatives; retain source IDs and meaningful explanations for explicit nonnumeric terms. Check included tooling has no zero candidate. A supplied explicit numeric zero control must remain valid for applicable costs.
3. **Conflicts:** source-paired money/integer alternatives with null primary and union IDs; existing lead-time text conflict; separate currency conflict. Mutate primary value, alternative type/value, duplicate values, missing explanation, missing/duplicate/out-of-document sources and incorrect union, and assert rejection where the existing contract owns that rule. Mutate a valid but wrong block association and verify the existing fixture scorer detects it rather than claiming membership validation detects semantic misattribution.
4. **Ten-case coverage:** validate and score all ten unchanged curated outputs provider-free, using their actual per-document source IDs. Add explicit controls for packaging money because the PDFs lack a known packaging amount. Preserve the quoted 5,000 quantity in the mismatch case, AUD in non-USD, sourced tooling unknown, missing fields and both conflict sources. These are curated/synthetic controls, not repaired live responses or new model results.
5. **Guidance and schema delivery:** inspect generated JSON schema descriptions for every numeric field and the shared alternative value; assert schema shape/types/requiredness are unchanged apart from descriptions. Check prompt coverage of all numeric keys, bounds, missing/included/conflict/currency rules and unchanged injection/tool restrictions. Existing injected-client runtime tests verify schema/instructions delivery, unchanged bounded repair, terminal rejection, no tools, session/document scope, and failure preservation. Updating a prompt-version expectation is bookkeeping, not proof that the model follows the prompt.
6. **Verification execution:** first run the targeted provider-free contract, schema, source, quote-domain, quote-eval, PDF and runner regressions; explicitly exclude reset-based database fixtures. Run API Ruff and Pyright. Run the existing full six-category offline baseline into a **new ignored working directory**, not retained historical evidence; expect 90/90 accounted for, 73 PASS and 17 EXPECTED_REJECTION. Check final consumed hashes and incremental scope, preserving pre-existing unrelated work. Do not weaken a scorer or regenerate curated answers to obtain green results.

Existing database-backed extractor/processing/correction tests remain useful for draft, evidence and confirmation behavior, but many legacy fixtures reset/migrate the test database. Select rollback-safe coverage only with an available, verified isolated target and appropriate authorization; do not run reset/migration fixtures under this plan. The previously recorded **28 database-dependent comparison checks remain pending**, due to connection timeouts and unapproved reset/migration fixture behavior. This plan does not clear that gap or authorize environment recovery. Any additional unavailable database checks must also be listed separately, not counted as passes.

### Acceptance, risks and live boundary

Implementation acceptance requires only the listed files to change; descriptions/prompt/version/hash bindings aligned; structural contract and all domain rules unchanged; the positive/negative matrix and applicable deterministic checks passing; all ten curated cases preserving values, states and sources; and no changes to candidate/current/confirmed authority. Review must verify there is no response coercion, guessed zero, currency loss, invented source/value, rounding, business-rule change or unrelated remediation. Browser QA remains not applicable for this internal prompt/schema-description change; persistence behavior is covered only to the extent actually exercised by safe tests.

The change is sufficient to communicate the existing numeric rules for these ten single-currency documents: the model currently sees generic scalar fields and no numeric guidance, while the authoritative validators already enforce the desired representations. Tests can establish guidance presence, shape compatibility, accepted/rejected payloads, deterministic scoring and unchanged runtime safeguards. They **cannot establish model compliance, improved live extraction, numeric accuracy, semantic source entailment or release readiness**. Strict JSON schema does not expose the domain's after-validator semantics to the model automatically. A prompt can still be ignored, and unrepresentable numeric text can still be mishandled.

A **subsequent separately authorized live evaluation** must use the existing configured model/runtime/preflight and a fresh evidence directory; do not rerun live calls under implementation approval alone. A filtered quote run would account for all 12 quote cases (ten provider-backed extractions and two parser rejections), label itself filtered, and report terminal AIRun/repair outcomes plus field/numeric/currency/unknown/conflict/source metrics with actual denominators. Desired remediation evidence is successful domain-valid extraction for all ten, unchanged parser rejections, correct amounts/integers/original currencies, preserved sourced unknowns and conflicts, and inspected source/qualifier fidelity. Preserve every failure and do not rerun until green. A quote-only run cannot satisfy the outstanding full-baseline requirement or establish other-category quality. Live reports need new prompt/source/dataset fingerprints; historical fingerprints describe the original inputs only.

No blocking contract ambiguity was found for these ten cases: included tooling already has a sourced UNKNOWN representation with uncertainty. The flat quote-level currency field cannot fully encode arbitrary mixed-currency line items or paired amount/currency conflict alternatives, and numeric fields cannot represent ranges or qualitative inclusion as amounts. Preserve such source information and uncertainty without guessing a winner or conversion. A richer typed commercial/inclusion/range or per-amount-currency representation would need separate explicit contract/design approval; it is not part of this numeric-instruction correction. Do not claim this plan solves those wider representations.

**Preserved limitations:** exact retained comparison-response replay remains PENDING because verified original persisted snapshots are unavailable; no substitute or reconstructed truth is used. Historical live baseline remains **ERROR, exit 2: 17 PASS, 18 EXPECTED_REJECTION, 48 QUALITY_FINDING, 7 RUNTIME_ERROR**. No historical classification, response, fingerprint or AIRun is changed. The **28 database checks remain pending**. All **ten human-review cases remain PENDING with null verdicts**. Comparison-validator independent review is passed, but none of these acceptance gaps is thereby cleared. No M6-U2, other production remediation, live call, implementation, Done Check or commit is authorized by this planning record.

**Approval requested:** the exact five-file prompt/description and deterministic-regression exception above, including only active request-hash rebinding. Stop here for explicit human approval before implementation.

#### Implemented result

- `quote_extractor.py`: prompt **`quote-extractor-v2`** and model-facing `Field(description=...)` metadata cover all five monetary fields, both integer fields, separate original currency and the shared candidate/alternative/state/source/uncertainty properties. Money uses finite decimal-only strings, at most four fractional places; unit price is positive and other costs nonnegative. Quantities/MOQs use positive JSON integers. The prompt preserves sourced UNKNOWN/null values, included-tooling wording without inferred zero, conditions, conflict alternatives and source relationships. It forbids FX conversion, USD defaulting, rounding, invented values/sources and treating document text as instructions; extraction never confirms a quote.
- `test_quote_extractor.py` and `test_quote_extraction_eval.py`: added **357 provider-free cases**. The matrix covers KNOWN and CONFLICT representations across every numeric key; valid scales/bounds and explicit zero; invalid currency/units/prose/types/bounds/precision/nonfinite amounts; UNKNOWN/null and optional sources; conflict structure and alternative-source guards; USD/AUD/EUR and unknown currency without amount conversion; included tooling; missing versus explicitly unstated values; all ten unchanged curated outputs and their request bindings; packaging controls; misattributed in-document sources detected by scoring; complete generated-schema compatibility; and actual extractor/structured-runtime behavior over a mocked persistence boundary.
- `evals/quote_extraction/cases.json`: changed **only the ten active `request_hash` values**, recomputed from final instructions and the unchanged numbered-block input serialization. Expected facts, curated model outputs, PDFs and parsed-block fixtures were preserved. This rebinding is for curated regression fixtures, not a revision to live evidence.
- This unit record documents authorization and results. No other nonignored file changed relative to the immediate pre-edit inventory.

No response coercion, normalized-return assignment, domain/business-rule change, new field/default/constraint, persistence change or runtime-limit change was introduced. Full production AST comparison, after removing only the `Field` import/description metadata and the two approved prompt constants, matches the pre-edit worktree. ### Approved eval-only provider-error diagnostics — 2026-11-02

Human approved implementation limited to the existing eval `CapturingClient`, directly related eval tests and this document. Status remains **`IN_REVIEW`**. This is an explicit exception to the earlier no-diagnostics implementation boundary; it does not authorize production tracing, M6-U2, provider calls or live reruns.

#### Exception-chain confirmation and behavior

The unchanged production `OpenAIResponsesClient` raises `ModelProviderError(...) from error` in both invocation methods. Provider-free tests inject actual SDK exception objects into the production wrapper's mocked Responses boundary and confirm that the SDK object remains accessible as the normalized exception's immediate `__cause__`. No production change was needed.

`CapturingClient` accepts optional keyword-only `provider_error_directory` and `case_id`. Both are needed to emit diagnostics: the directory must resolve beneath the already-ignored `apps/api/.eval-results/` tree, and the case must exactly match a known local quote-extraction case ID. Defaults remain disabled. No environment setting, CLI option, baseline runner factory change or automatic capture was introduced. A separately authorized evaluation must explicitly inject these constructor arguments through the existing eval client seam; this implementation does not run or enable such an evaluation.

On `ModelProviderError`, capture reads only the immediate SDK exception's status/type/code/parameter attributes. It does not inspect messages, bodies, requests, responses, headers, identifiers, stack traces or configuration. Each failed wrapper invocation creates a fresh, exclusive `provider-error-*.json` sidecar containing exactly:

- `case_id`: validated local quote case ID;
- `invocation`: one-based `CapturingClient` invocation count, including successful calls and both invocation methods; not an SDK HTTP retry count;
- `http_status`: integer HTTP status when available, otherwise null;
- `provider_error_type`, `provider_error_code`, `rejected_parameter`: exact finite-allowlist matches, null when unavailable, otherwise `UNRECOGNIZED` (including malformed non-string values).

Parameter allowlisting covers the existing request's top-level identifiers and `text.format` identifiers through `text.format.schema`; unfamiliar/deeper parameter paths are deliberately not retained. Provider type/code allowlists cover bounded authentication, permission, model access, request/schema, quota/rate and service-error identifiers. Unknown values are never copied or interpreted as a known rejection reason. No request or schema payload is captured.

Original provider exceptions are re-raised unchanged. Diagnostic inspection, directory creation and file-write failures are best-effort and cannot replace the provider exception; exception details are never logged. A failed write may leave an empty or partial sanitized sidecar and is not evidence of successful capture. Sidecars are separate from baseline reports and AIRun records, never overwrite an existing file, and add no retry, model call, runtime limit or status/classification behavior. Non-provider exceptions pass through unchanged.

#### Verification and preservation

Provider-free synthetic coverage in `tests/test_eval_provider_diagnostics.py` verifies HTTP 400/401/403/404/422, unavailable and malformed statuses, known/unknown provider identifiers, missing/non-SDK causes, unexpected attribute access, exact field allowlisting and secret-sentinel exclusion, both invocation methods, successful-call numbering, fresh-file preservation, invalid case IDs, output-path restrictions, default-disabled behavior, and metadata/directory/file-write failures. Enabled/disabled runtime comparisons verify identical exceptions, outputs, provider retry and schema-repair counts, captured responses, validation state, case accounting and exit classification, including diagnostic-write failures.

Implementation verification evidence is kept separately in the ignored `apps/api/.eval-results/provider-error-diagnostics-2026-11-02/` directory.
