### Approved eval-only Quote Extraction scorer correction — 2026-11-03

**Authorization:** the user approved the seven-file remediation plan, expressly retained Harbor `supplier_notes=UNKNOWN`, prohibited dataset/production/runtime changes and required `IN_REVIEW` throughout. This correction implements that authorization only. The historical 34/43 finding and all earlier baseline evidence remain unchanged.

#### Implementation and bounded semantics

- `quote_extraction_eval.py`: transient parsed-document context binds the selected PDF filename and SHA-256 to all ordered actual parsed IDs/text through the existing extraction request hash. Missing, incomplete, wrong-PDF, changed-content and duplicate-ID contexts raise `QuoteScorerContextError`. No expected-citation subset supplies membership. The runner attaches context after actual parsing, clears stale context before a new parse, and retains the expected unsupported-PDF rejection branch.
- `eval_scoring.py`: production source-reference validation uses all actual block IDs, including conflict alternatives. Field evidence is checked independently. Exact evidence remains the default; conflict alternative-to-source pairing remains checked. Named field assertions and aggregate metrics share the same field evaluation, including conflict state and alternative-to-evidence pairing. Missing context is `SCORER_ERROR` in offline scoring and the live runner boundary, not a quality finding. The live boundary is verified with mocked infrastructure and provider construction forbidden; no live case was run.
- Only Harbor's reviewed exclusion capitalization, qualified `GRS` representation and USD evidence alternatives are accepted. Currency evidence is a nonempty subset of the four exact reviewed USD blocks. `GRS` requires exact `p2-b4` text/citation and the retained explicit synthetic/current/fictional uncertainty sentence. Missing or changed qualification, added verification, changed negation and irrelevant citations remain rejected. Rule identifiers, actual/expected representations, qualification and evidence are retained in assertion details. There is no general fuzzy/semantic matching.
- Harbor's exact sourced synthetic disclaimer, without extra uncertainty and with matching disclaimer evidence, is a critical `field.supplier_notes.field_assignment` error. Its field/UNKNOWN/source correctness failures remain. This does not change the expected fixture or define supplier notes generally. Cross-field exact expected facts with their exact evidence can be identified as assignment errors; numeric fact support across integer/decimal field types does not make the destination field correct.
- Unsupported numeric/currency values create critical `field.<key>.unsupported_value` findings, including replacements of expected-KNOWN values. Unrecognized text creates critical `field.<key>.unresolved_support` findings, not an automated claim of either support or proven invention. Correct expected values with wrong citations create critical `misattributed_evidence` findings. An unresolved or misplaced value cannot become a passing case merely because its information or ID exists in the document.

Metric definitions and denominators are documented in `apps/api/evals/README.md`. The original seven aggregate assertion names are retained. `hallucination_rate` now counts bounded unsupported KNOWN numeric/currency values within expected-UNKNOWN fields / expected-UNKNOWN fields; it is not an exhaustive free-text invention measure. The former numerator is separately preserved as `expected_unknown_known_rate`. `unsupported_value_rate` and `unresolved_support_rate` each use all 21 fields, including expected-KNOWN replacements. Diagnostic rates are measurements, while per-field diagnostic findings remain critical exact assertions. Zero hallucination does not establish free-text groundedness; unresolved text remains failing. Other original field/numeric/currency/UNKNOWN/conflict/source denominators are unchanged.

#### Regression evidence and local Harbor rescore

Positive/negative controls cover complete membership outside expected citations; nonexistent and irrelevant IDs; wrong-document context including overlapping IDs; changed PDF digest and block content; incomplete/duplicate blocks; swapped conflict evidence; correct and incorrect currency alternatives; qualified/unqualified GRS and added claims; case-only exclusions and changed negation; UNKNOWN versus invented KNOWN numerics; wrong-field source-supported facts; invented prices/costs and unresolved warranty/notes text. Non-Harbor controls prove no new equivalence or disclaimer exception applies to another case. Missing-context runner controls retain both expected parser rejections and prohibit provider construction. Report tests check named findings and numerator/denominator consistency in persisted JSON and readable summaries.

#### Critical findings and bounded interpretation

The unmodified report contains **359/443 exact assertions passing and 84 failed critical assertions**, including two passing parser assertions. Of the 84 failures, **27 are additional named diagnostics**: 18 `unresolved_support` and nine `misattributed_evidence`. These overlap underlying field/aggregate failures and are not 84 independent extraction errors. All ten cases fail the aggregate `field_accuracy`, `unknown_detection` and `source_block_accuracy` assertions. All exact names/details remain in the report; per-field actual/expected values and actual source text are retained in `inspection.json`.

- **QE-F01 — expected-UNKNOWN supplier-notes assignment:** all ten outputs put the source-supported synthetic-demo disclaimer in `supplier_notes`, where the oracle expects UNKNOWN. Harbor's approved UNKNOWN interpretation makes its destination-field error confirmed; documentary support means it is not invented text. The unchanged scorer reports `unresolved_support` even for this Harbor output because its uppercase source wording differs from the exact previously reviewed title-case assignment exception. This is a bounded diagnostic-recognition limitation; the UNKNOWN failure remains valid. Other cases also have confirmed expected-state mismatches, while broader supplier-notes semantics remain unresolved rather than silently generalized from Harbor. All ten disclaimer texts are sourced; Unicode dash/capitalization variants are preserved in the JSON.
- **QE-F02 — same-currency evidence-set limitation:** all nine non-Harbor cases cite multiple real blocks explicitly stating the correct USD or AUD currency. The exact source oracle accepts only the unit-price block outside Harbor, causing `field.currency.sources` and `field.currency.misattributed_evidence`. Inspection confirms these extra IDs support the same currency; none is foreign, nonexistent or irrelevant to currency. Thus the official failures are retained, but actual wrong-source attribution is **not independently confirmed**. Harbor's reviewed currency alternatives pass.
- **QE-F03 — exclusion wording limitation:** six cases (`missing-shipping`, `included-tooling`, `missing-moq`, `quantity-mismatch`, `non-usd`, `explicit-unknown`) return `Import duties are not included` instead of lowercase `import...`, with correct `p2-b7` evidence and unchanged negation. The explicit capitalization equivalence remains Harbor-only, so these retain `.value` and `.unresolved_support` failures. No changed exclusion meaning was observed; no scorer broadening or acceptance override occurred.
- **QE-F04 — certification wording and compliance assignment:** `quantity-mismatch` uses `Synthetic GRS record; current for this fictional supplier` in both certifications and compliance notes, citing `p2-b4`. Certification differs by semicolon versus expected colon, with the synthetic/current/fictional qualification retained. Compliance notes is expected UNKNOWN, so its duplicate placement contributes the eleventh UNKNOWN mismatch and an expected-field assignment discrepancy. Both officially remain `unresolved_support`; whether that qualification should additionally populate compliance notes requires human direction. No invented certification or real-world verification is established. All other certification values pass, including Harbor's full exact source value.

The 20 field-source mismatches comprise nine currency evidence-set differences, ten supplier-notes placements and one compliance-notes placement. All 210 fields nevertheless pass document membership at output level. No unsupported money/quantity/currency, lost positive conflict, invented source ID or terminal execution fault was found. The notes/compliance expected-state discrepancies remain material; inspection does not turn them into passes.

There are no quote heuristic-signal assertions in this report. Exact deterministic checks still use a bounded oracle rather than exhaustive semantic entailment. Zero bounded hallucination is not proof of general free-text groundedness. The distinctions above preserve official classification while separating confirmed destination/state discrepancies, documented exact-oracle limitations and unresolved general field semantics. No human-review verdict was manufactured.

#### Reconciliation with official metrics and completion

The report remains **COMPLETED_WITH_FINDINGS**, `complete=true`, `full_scope=false`, **12/12 accounted for**, **exit 0**, with ten QUALITY_FINDING and two EXPECTED_REJECTION cases. All ten AIRuns are COMPLETED/VALID; neither PDF rejection requires a model call. `unsupported-image-only` and `malformed-pdf` each pass their one expected-rejection assertion. There is no evidence here for changing execution status or exit code. Exit 0 establishes trustworthy selected-scope completion, not quality acceptance or the full six-category release baseline.

**84 failed assertions = 30 aggregate failures + 27 named field failures + 27 additional diagnostics.** The 27 distinct case/field differences are **10 notes + 9 currency source sets + 6 exclusions + 1 certification + 1 compliance destination**. These are not 27 confirmed model errors. T7 changes the interpretation of one diagnostic; T8 adds presentation observations within two already-counted notes fields. Official totals stay **357/441** on ten extractions plus **2/2** parser checks = **359/443** overall. No adjusted/pass-after-triage metric is asserted.

| Official measurement | Reconciliation and limitation |
| --- | --- |
| Field accuracy **192/210** | 18 value/state mismatches = 10 notes + 6 exclusion casing + 1 certification punctuation + 1 compliance destination. Sources are measured separately for KNOWN fields; positive conflict matching also requires source pairing. |
| Numeric **55/55** | Expected KNOWN numeric fields only: six each ordinarily, five in missing shipping/included tooling/missing MOQ, four in explicit unknown. Does not certify absent-value handling or qualifications by itself. |
| Currency **10/10** | Nine USD, one AUD correct; currency-value metric does not check field attribution. |
| UNKNOWN **74/85**; expected-UNKNOWN KNOWN **11/85** | Ten notes plus one compliance destination. UNKNOWN states for genuine absent/explicitly unstated commercial facts survive. Only Harbor notes is a confirmed approved assignment error; the remaining ten destination decisions are unresolved. |
| Conflict **1/1** | One positive production-lead-time conflict, alternatives correctly paired. Other cases' 0/0 conflict assertions are vacuous passes, not extra positive coverage. |
| Field-source **190/210** | 20 expected-set differences = nine supported currency sets + ten notes placements + one compliance placement. This is exact-or-bounded expected-citation agreement, not 20 proven wrong citations. |
| Complete membership **10/10 outputs** | Every returned primary/alternative ID belongs to its actual PDF. This does not certify field assignment, semantic entailment, commercial completeness or correct pairing; those were separately inspected. |
| Bounded hallucination **0/85**, unsupported value **0/210** | No numeric/currency invention detected under this bounded classifier. Not a free-text hallucination guarantee: arbitrary mismatched prose is classified unresolved. |
| Unresolved support **18/210** | 10 disclaimer placements + 6 exclusion casing + 1 certification punctuation + 1 compliance placement. Triage resolves support for the words; it does not automatically approve their normalized destination. The nine additional `misattributed_evidence` diagnostics are the currency-set limitations. |

The scorer's tests intentionally limit Harbor equivalences to Harbor; the observed false positives follow that bounded design rather than proving a regression in the approved implementation. Default text comparison is exact, source comparison is an expected set, and known-value aggregate scoring does not establish uncertainty completeness. Explicit-UNKNOWN source/uncertainty preservation and commercial qualifiers therefore needed this independent PDF inspection. The aggregate assertion `unknown_detection` fails in every case because of notes, even though critical commercial UNKNOWN controls pass.

#### Remaining release risk and recommended disposition

1. **P2 model issue:** Harbor's approved UNKNOWN is replaced by a sourced document notice. Keep it visible and require a disposition before quote-quality acceptance. A narrow assignment correction is the only confirmed model-behavior remediation justified now; no numeric/currency/conflict prompt change is supported by this evidence.

#### Approved decisions and exact evidence scope

Evidence: [completed 12-case baseline](evidence/M6-U1/quote-v2-filtered-live-2026-11-03-10d48e60/baseline/results.json), [inspection](evidence/M6-U1/quote-v2-filtered-live-2026-11-03-10d48e60/inspection.json), the original ten extractable PDFs and expected parsed blocks. The preceding read-only analysis independently matched actual parsed blocks to expected blocks, checked request bindings and equality of captured response JSON with structured outputs. The two expected parser rejections are unaffected.

| Decision | Approved interpretation and bounded application |
| --- | --- |
| A1 — ten supplier-notes placements | Every repeated `SYNTHETIC DEMO QUOTE - NOT A REAL SUPPLIER OFFER` notice is fixture metadata. Expected `supplier_notes` stays UNKNOWN/null/empty sources for `harbor-primary`, `willow-primary`, `moss-primary`, `missing-shipping`, `conflicting-lead-time`, `included-tooling`, `missing-moq`, `quantity-mismatch`, `non-usd`, `explicit-unknown`. Sourced extraction there is `field_assignment`, not hallucination. This does not define general supplier-note behavior or accept the field as correct. |
| A2 — compliance duplication | Only `quantity-mismatch.compliance_notes`: retain UNKNOWN/null/empty sources. KNOWN `Synthetic GRS record; current for this fictional supplier`, citing `p2-b4`, duplicates the certification fact and is a fixture-specific `field_assignment`. Preserve the full synthetic/current/fictional qualification in `certifications`; do not infer real verification or prohibit duplication generally. |
| A3 — currency attribution | Accept the nine documented full alternative citation sets below only for the correct original currency, correct PDF and exact supporting text. Preserve existing Harbor acceptance. Membership alone never establishes attribution. |
| A4 — text equivalence | Accept lowercase-to-initial-uppercase `import duties are not included` in the six documented cases, with `p2-b7` text `Exclusion: import duties are not included`. Accept the colon-to-semicolon variant of the full qualified certification only in `quantity-mismatch.certifications`, with `p2-b4`. Preserve negation, all qualifications and source checks. No general casefolding or punctuation stripping. |
| A5 — Unicode correction | Willow retains `Synthetic demo quote \u2014 not a real supplier offer.`; quantity mismatch retains `Synthetic demo quote\u2014not a real supplier offer`. Here `\u2014` denotes the actual Unicode em dash. Both captured `output_text` and structured output contain U+2014; report bytes contain UTF-8 `E2 80 94`, not U+00E2/U+20AC/U+201D. No retained-text corruption is demonstrated. The earlier display artifact's exact origin remains UNRESOLVED. No encoding repair, parser fix or evidence normalization is justified. |

For A1, each notice source is the exact uppercase fixture notice. Actual source sets are `p1-b1` for Harbor, Willow, Moss and explicit-unknown; `p1-b1,p2-b1` for the other six. Preserve the previously approved Harbor title-case diagnostic form as well.

All A3 cases expect `p1-b5` alone and return that block plus the extras below. Every listed extra explicitly states the same currency; none is irrelevant. Amounts identify the evidence text, not extra numeric acceptance rules.

#### Proposal A — eval-only correction, pending separate implementation approval

**Smallest proposed scope: exactly five existing files.** No new generic semantic scorer, dependency, fixture, production rule or report schema. Recognize A2's exact qualified duplicate as assignment only for quantity mismatch. Add A3's nine complete citation-set alternatives, order-insensitive, guarded by case/PDF identity, original currency, exact expected source text and existing complete parsed-document/request binding. Preserve exact original source sets and existing Harbor rules. Add only A4's seven value aliases, with their exact source text/IDs and retained qualifications. Keep altered/unknown prose unresolved and unsupported numeric/currency values unsupported. |

Prefer accepting only each documented non-Harbor full alternative set plus its original expected set; accepting arbitrary additional subsets would exceed the smallest required change. Existing Harbor's reviewed subset rule remains unchanged. Context still requires matching selected PDF filename/digest, complete ordered blocks and request hash. Bounded rules must additionally verify their relevant source text; a real block or an overlapping ID from another PDF is insufficient. Diagnostic recognition must not excuse unsupported added claims or nonnull uncertainty containing new claims.

#### Optional production clarification — deferred, not included in Proposal A

There are eleven confirmed fixture assignment errors that a production prompt clarification might address. Candidate wording would distinguish this synthetic-demo authenticity notice from supplier facts and keep this complete synthetic/current/fictional GRS statement in certifications without duplicating it into compliance notes when no separate compliance fact is stated. It must not suppress genuine supplier-specific conditions or prohibit certification/compliance overlap generally. No parser, encoding, numeric, currency, schema or runtime correction is supported by this adjudication.

The direct candidate files would be `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/quote_extractor.py` (instructions and a new prompt version), `apps/api/tests/test_quote_extractor.py` (captured-instruction, qualification, real-supplier-note and review-only invariants), and this unit document. However, this is **not a self-contained three-file implementation proposal under the current constraints**: `structured_input_hash()` includes `INSTRUCTIONS`; both parsed-context validation and recorded-output replay rely on all ten existing `cases.json` request hashes. Changing instructions would invalidate those bindings even with unchanged PDF bytes. Merely changing the version, silently rehashing fixtures, bypassing binding checks or using an old prompt with new provenance would not solve this correctly.

**Recommendation: approve Proposal A only and defer production changes.** A future separately approved production plan must first resolve versioned request/fixture provenance, identify its resulting exact file scope, preserve all historical fixtures/reports and maintain honest separation between old recorded outputs and new-prompt evaluation. No request-binding framework, fixture edit or production prompt change is proposed or authorized here. The current no-fixture-change boundary remains absolute for this proposal. Any future prompt evaluation needs explicit authorization; provider-free recorded responses alone cannot demonstrate changed live model behavior.

#### Risks, preservation and approval boundary

- Overbroad aliases could hide irrelevant evidence, changed negation, stripped qualifications or unsupported additions. Mitigation: explicit fixture/content/source guards and paired negative controls; keep all complete-document and conflict-pairing validation.
- Semantic approval must not become a pass override. Mitigation: retain eleven state/source disagreements and critical assignment findings, plus all original commercial UNKNOWN controls.
- Changed assertion denominators could suggest artificial quality gains. Mitigation: show unchanged historical metrics alongside clearly labeled local scorer projections/results and diagnostic-count reconciliation.
- Earlier corruption wording is historically inaccurate. This entry explicitly supersedes it; do not erase prior evidence or normalize retained bytes. Earlier display-artifact origin stays unresolved.
- Existing historical reports, PDFs, expected blocks/outputs/request hashes, human-review inputs and all ten PENDING/null verdicts are protected. This task authorizes documentation only. Proposal A requires separate implementation approval; optional production work requires a separate provenance-aware plan.

Read-only consistency checks confirmed historical 359/443 assertions, all ten PENDING/null review records and preservation of the earlier superseded corruption statement. Documentation whitespace/structure checks passed. No implementation tests or evaluations were run for this planning-only edit.

**Stop:** adjudications recorded and plan prepared only. M6-U1 remains **`IN_REVIEW`**. No scorer/production implementation, fixture/expected-output change, report rewrite, provider call, live rerun, M6-U2, Done Check or commit.

### Distinct Brief issues

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

Preserve numeric direction and REQUIRED; do not default all requirements to REQUIRED or add a general language rules engine. | Paired mandatory/preferred/unstated cases for lead time, MOQ, certification and branding; assert importance and exact value independently. | B2 — P1 blocking runtime failure: valid nullable periods rejected by deterministic validation | `brief/delivery-period` and `brief/relative-delivery` final responses contain a DELIVERY/PERIOD requirement with `kind`, `label`, `start_date=null`, `end_date=null`. Preserved triage calls these schema-invalid, citing missing exact period keys. | **Confirmed current production defect by static inspection:** `ExtractedRequirement.validate_domain_value` calls `expected_value.model_dump(exclude_none=True)`, removing both endpoint keys; domain `validate_delivery_target` requires all four keys and explicitly permits nullable endpoints. This is not proven model misconduct. Narrow deterministic validation correction: preserve the required nullable keys for `PeriodTarget` only; retain omission of optional null currency for `PriceValue`. No schema relaxation or date inference. Historical QUALITY_FINDING labels remain unchanged. | Accept label-only, one-endpoint and two-endpoint periods through the actual ExtractedBrief validation/structured-operation path; reject malformed dates, wrong kinds and extra keys; check UNIT_PRICE with absent currency still works. Keep top-level and requirement period validation consistent. |
| B3 — P1 critical model-quality error: uncertainty converted into evaluable requirement | `brief/ambiguous-material` emits MATERIAL `cotton or polyester` while also saying material is undecided. | Accepted output can feed a single-text material evaluator with an unresolved choice. Preserve the ambiguity without manufacturing a settled material requirement. Production Brief guidance belongs with B1; do not silently pick cotton/polyester, create OR-rule infrastructure or weaken scoring. | Retain the original undecided-material case; pair with definite cotton and definite polyester, and an explicitly stated material requirement. Assert ambiguity and absence of a falsely settled requirement. |
| B4 — P2 model-quality/availability findings, with unresolved representation policy | Product name 11/21 and category 9/21: emitted null despite explicit bag/pouch/bottle wording; see named failures in the full summary. `branding-unknown` moves “Branding has not been decided” to BRANDING_NOTES and leaves ambiguities empty. `price-currency` returns informational subject/text under UNIT_PRICE rather than amount/currency, leaves the explicit target price null, and fails repair. | Missing stated core fields and the invalid price shape are genuine findings, not aliases. Clarify Brief guidance with B1: retain stated product/category and supported value shapes; keep undecided branding visible. Whether the preserved branding attribute alone is an acceptable uncertainty representation needs adjudication before changing its expected ambiguity check. Price “below” has an existing bounded threshold fixture; do not invent a new comparison operator or erase the qualification. | Cover stated generic product/category versus truly absent fields; price amount `6`/AUD/REQUIRED, optional missing price/currency, mandatory versus preferred bounds; uncertain branding versus explicit false/true. Keep user approval and all deterministic readiness/domain checks. |
| B5 — P2 scorer limitation / acceptable recorded quality finding, not fabricated facts | `complete-demo`, `missing-destination`, `missing-delivery`, `missing-quantity`, `no-price`, `branding-unknown`, `informational-color`, `informational-packaging` add requirements grounded in the request beyond fixture expectations. `ambiguous-delivery` retains the uncertain period and a differently worded ambiguity. `explicit-moq` and `complete-demo` record representational caveats about bounds. `non-invention` records “for our new brand” as BRANDING_NOTES. | Exact list/wording mismatches need field-specific adjudication. Do not count these collectively as hallucinations: historical absent-field metric 14/100 is not 14 invented facts. Proposed eval work is an approved case-level representation policy/aliases only where semantics actually match. Keep B1/B3 and omitted core fields failing. The branding-context placement is unresolved, not an automatic synonym. ### Supplier evidence and claim reliability

**S1 — P1 blocking runtime failure / critical evidence-selection quality error, still unresolved.** In `supplier_explanation/clear-match` and `/conflict`, the model calls the correct tool name, `get_supplier_evidence`, but passes the **requirement UUID in `claim_keys`**, not an evidence claim key. The adapter creates claim keys from finding kinds (`MATERIAL`/`CERTIFICATION` in these cases); the tool performs an exact key filter. Thus the request is syntactically valid yet selects no evidence. Initial final outputs cite an attached evidence UUID without having fetched it; repair then removes citations rather than fetching the correct evidence, producing `Point omits available requirement evidence.` Both findings are supported by captured arguments/finals plus the retained semantic replay. This is filter misuse and unfetched provenance, not proof that the evidence ID was invented or the evidence is missing from the fixture.

The deterministic guard correctly prevents unsupported presentation. Smallest proposed production change: explain in the supplier evidence tool description/instructions that `claim_keys` are returned catalog claim keys, never requirement/evidence IDs; use null when exact keys are not known. Retain attached-evidence and fetched-evidence checks, source scope, one repair, and existing limits. Do not map arbitrary UUIDs to claims, fall back silently to all evidence inside the tool, auto-attach citations or bypass validation. Tests should cover null returning real scoped evidence, exact-key filtering, requirement-UUID filtering returning empty, initial unfetched citation rejection, empty repair rejection, and a valid corrected evidence fetch/final within the same four-turn/six-tool budget.

**S2 — P2 scorer limitation / unresolved free-text quality, no confirmed new accepted hallucination.** Four supplier live cases completed (`grounded-mixed-states`, `required-failure`, `unknown`, `near-match`) and required states are rendered by the application. Five adversarial supplier controls reject as expected. These do not certify arbitrary evidence-only concern prose: ID validity alone does not prove entailment. The difficult `unsupported-supplier-action` and `supported-capability-paraphrase` examples remain human-review inputs, not new actual model failures or completed judgments. Retain the bounded scorer, mutation tests and pending human review; no new semantic parser/AI judge/production validator is justified by this evidence alone.

Their requested batches exceed remaining tool capacity; requests after the sixth are blocked. There is no evidence of a timeout, provider outage, database failure or an off-by-one rejection of the sixth successful call. Snapshot/evidence rereads consume the same budget. Current prompts do not give an explicit economical lookup plan or explain the remaining budget. Existing six-call-success and retry/turn tests demonstrate guard behavior, not model efficiency.

### Outstanding blockers and full-baseline readiness

**P1 release blockers:** B1 mandatory importance loss; B2 deterministic rejection of valid period requirements; B3 unresolved choice becoming an evaluable material constraint; S1 evidence selection/repair failures; A1 widespread tool-budget exhaustion and independent invalid conflict sections; and the unresolved `corrected-quote-provenance` dispatch failure. The six semantic-error RUNTIME_ERROR cases are not six new framework/runtime crashes: two are supplier provenance failures, four involve addressed binding defects with independent invalid content still visible. None has a new accepted live result. P1 here means important correctness/availability must be addressed or explicitly dispositioned before release, not that failed content escaped guards.

**P2 findings/decisions:** omitted core fields, price shape/prompt ambiguity and branding uncertainty handling, exact Brief representation/scorer limitations, authentic historical replay gaps, and all ten pending narrative reviews. Scorer findings and review-pending prose must not be used to dilute the P1 issues. No new P0 data-loss/security incident is established by this evidence.

**Ready for one fresh full six-category live baseline now? No, as a release-validation step.** Database readiness and reviewed quote/scorer corrections remove their earlier blockers, but do not remove the confirmed non-quote defect or the unchanged evidence/budget patterns. It would be technically possible to collect another explicitly authorized diagnostic baseline and retain the same failures; that is distinct from readiness and is not recommended before the bounded corrections. Do not require every recorded wording-quality finding to disappear before collecting trustworthy baseline evidence.

Recommended gates: obtain approval for N1–N4; implement and independently review only those approved exceptions; run their targeted deterministic/contract/tool/scorer regressions and a fresh provider-free full baseline under separate authorization; resolve the dispatch diagnosis or explicitly carry its uncertainty with a mandatory new-case inspection plan; retain optional N5 adjudications separately. Then request authorization for **one fresh full six-category** live run with fresh unchanged dedicated-eval isolation/schema preflight, current input/prompt hashes, existing retries/limits, fresh output directory and full case accounting. Do not retry until green or promote a filtered quote run to full acceptance. Compare outcomes against preserved historical evidence, report any remaining execution errors separately from model quality, and leave human reviews pending until performed. Release acceptance and Done Check remain separate decisions.

Negative cases cover every absent period key, impossible/non-ISO endpoint dates, wrong period and requirement kinds, extra keys, blank labels, absent price/schema currency keys, malformed/zero/negative amounts, invalid currency case/length/type, extra price keys and an informational value wrongly used for UNIT_PRICE. Existing ignored historical working artifacts were not outputs of this invocation. Runner and subsequent existing hash checks confirm consumed source/input preservation. Evidence was written only to the fresh directory above; this document is the intentional post-run results update.

**Recommendation: BLOCKED.** All six categories and all 90 cases are inspectable, metrics and critical findings are recorded, but **20 execution errors prevent the required trustworthy full-live-baseline acceptance**. Brief/quote correctness findings and pending narrative review retain their stated limitations; they must not be conflated with environment/provider outages or waived by case-accounting completeness. Stop here: M6-U1 **IN_REVIEW**; Done Check not started; no automatic remediation, additional live evaluation, staging, commit or M6-U2.

## Focused diagnosis of 20 terminal Analyst validation failures — 2026-11-03

### Evidence and investigation boundary

Used the retained [90-case results and both attempted finals](evidence/M6-U1/final-full-live-2026-11-03-01/baseline/results.json), [ordered trace/validation inspection](evidence/M6-U1/final-full-live-2026-11-03-01/inspection.json), current production validators/runtime/prompts, and existing Analyst tests. Read the authentic persisted comparison payloads by the 20 retained AIRun IDs using SELECTs in explicitly READ ONLY transactions against the existing eval database. Reproduced both finals' first exceptions with the unchanged validators and inspected their rejected point/local assertion; inspected other points and section references separately to expose masked failures. These secondary checks do not turn an invalid final into an accepted result. UTF-8 source outputs were used; all consumed-file hashes still match the retained baseline provenance.

No provider calls, evaluation runs, fixture writes, database changes, new scripts/infrastructure, historical rescoring, tests, or production edits. Only this document changes. Product evidence/uncertainty rules, Architecture sections 8/15.10/19.2, and M6-U1's failure-inspection scope remain controlling. N1–N3, quote scorer, database work and the historical supplier-snapshot incident were not reopened.

### Exact terminal failure groups

Paths are zero-based. Suggestion cases have an additional `analysis.` prefix. All eleven suggestion cases fail in `validate_advice → validate_analysis`, before `validate_suggestion` executes. Counts below are mutually exclusive and total 20.

| Cases | Exact validator / invariant / rejected output field | Confirmed cause | Recommended action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **17: nine comparison + eight suggestion** | `tradeoff_analysis._validate_state_assertions`, line 381: `Requirement-state assertion has an unsupported topic or supplier.` Unconsumed words remain after supplier/target/state parsing. `supplier_summaries[0].text`. | Closed assertion grammar rejects attribution prose such as `is marked MEETS`, `comparison marks … as MEETS`, `is evaluated as MEETS`, `according to …`. Snapshot A certification is MEETS in these cases; the first rejection is not proof of a false state claim. Comparison `mixed-currencies` and `required-conflict` also use `GRS requirement` without the recognized certification-kind label; `incomplete-evidence` embeds a UUID and a non-leading supplier. | Give explicit, locally bound canonical state-sentence examples, with exact supplier name, requirement kind and state; separate numeric/provenance explanations. Preserve the parser's rejection of unresolved topics/owners. |
| **1: suggestion/required-failure** | Same function/line/message; `analysis.key_tradeoffs[0].text`. | Repair fixes the first summary, but `whereas Supplier B does not meet it` leaves `whereas Supplier B` unconsumed: supplier recognition only consumes the leading subject slot. B's certification failure is supported; the clause construction is unsupported. | Repeat the supplier and certification label in an independent sentence/point instead of conjunction/pronoun binding. |
| **1: suggestion/critical-unknown** | `_validate_state_assertions`, line 392: `Requirement-state assertion has ambiguous references.` `analysis.supplier_summaries[0].text`. | Both attempts say `Its required GRS certification is UNKNOWN` after a full stop. Sentence boundary resets owner context; `Its` disables the single-cited-supplier fallback. A's certification really is UNKNOWN. | Explicitly name the supplier in each state sentence. Do not restore cross-sentence ownership or weaken ambiguity rejection. |
| **1: suggestion/tie** | `validate_analysis`, line 599: `UNKNOWN point contradicts comparison completeness.` `analysis.important_unknowns[1].quote_fields[0]` references A's `unit_price`. | Repair removes the initial pronoun issue but introduces an UNKNOWN point for the corrected price's unverified provenance. Its completeness is **known**, not missing. True provenance caution is not a deterministic UNKNOWN field. | Keep that caution and its citation in summaries/tradeoffs; reserve UNKNOWN-section references for missing fields/UNKNOWN requirements. Keep the validator unchanged. |

The 17-case group's comparison IDs are `meaningful-tradeoff`, `required-failure`, `unknown-and-conflict`, `corrected-quote-provenance`, `incomplete-evidence`, `general-vs-project`, `mixed-currencies`, `unequal-quantities`, `required-conflict`. Its suggestion IDs are `clear-suggestion`, `incomplete-evidence`, `user-correction`, `incomparable-prices`, `general-vs-project`, `mixed-currencies`, `unequal-quantities`, `required-conflict`.

### Initial versus repaired outputs; why repair did not complete

Initial first exceptions are **16 unsupported-topic/supplier, three ambiguous-reference, one unknown-quote-field**; repaired exceptions are **18 unsupported-topic/supplier, one ambiguous-reference, one UNKNOWN-completeness**. All 40 finals are schema-valid. Each case has one successful lookup batch, initial final, one REPAIR, repaired final, then VALIDATION_FAILED: three model turns, no limit termination or invalid tool.

- Sixteen initial grammar failures remain grammar failures. Most repairs substitute another unsupported attribution verb. `suggestion/required-failure` advances from summary to tradeoff but does not produce a valid whole answer.
- Initial `comparison/incomplete-evidence` and `suggestion/tie` use `It MEETS …` after a full stop. Repair replaces the former with unsupported attribution/embedded-reference prose; the latter gets through state parsing but adds the invalid provenance UNKNOWN point. `suggestion/critical-unknown` repeats its exact problematic sentence.
- Initial `suggestion/required-conflict` fails `validate_analysis`, line 569, at `analysis.supplier_summaries[0].quote_fields[3].quote_id`: the lead-time citation invents `d5a8a820-b331-4f2f-d41a-2ea08daa7f4b` instead of the actual `d5a8a820-b331-4f42-9f2b-d41a2ea08daa`. Its field key is legitimate; the quote ID is not. Repair corrects the ID but introduces/reaches `Supplier A is marked MEETS …`. This initial rejection is a correct provenance safeguard, not a schema defect.
- **Shared repair-context defect:** `run_comparison_analyst` (lines 794–810) parses `invocation.output_text`, then on failure only appends `Repair the invalid final result: {error}` to instructions. Unlike tool-call outputs, the invalid final is never appended to `input_items`; no continuation ID supplies it either. Repair therefore gets the original snapshot/tool history and a generic first error, without the answer to edit, its rejected field path, or canonical grammar guidance. This is a confirmed context omission, not proof that providing the answer alone would make all repairs succeed. The first-failure exception also hides later independent errors. Raising repair/turn limits is not justified.

### Additional failures masked by the first exception

These counts overlap the terminal groups and each other; they must not be added to 20. They are retained-output reference/state comparisons, not new runtime outcomes.

| Repaired cases | Rejected fields / deterministic invariant | Assessment and action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **16** (all nine comparison; suggestion `clear-suggestion`, `critical-unknown`, `incomplete-evidence`, `incomparable-prices`, `general-vs-project`, `mixed-currencies`, `unequal-quantities`) | `important_conflicts[0].quote_fields` cites A's **known** `production_lead_time` alongside its genuinely CONFLICT requirement. `validate_analysis` lines 589–599 requires every section citation to have the section's state. | Legitimate deterministic rejection. Requirement conflict does not make the related numeric field conflicting. Keep the requirement citation in CONFLICT; move the known-field explanation/citation to a summary/tradeoff. Initial outputs have this problem in 19 cases; repairs remove it in only three. |
| **7** (comparison `meaningful-tradeoff`, `required-failure`, `unknown-and-conflict`, `corrected-quote-provenance`, `incomplete-evidence`; suggestion `tie`, `incomparable-prices`) | `important_unknowns[*].quote_fields` includes known `unit_price` fields because currency or correction verification is unavailable. Comparison `required-failure` additionally cites A's CONFLICT lead requirement and known lead field in UNKNOWN. | Legitimate section-membership rejection. Missing currency/source verification is not missing numeric price. Do not manufacture UNKNOWN/CONFLICT states or suppress the caution; disclose it outside the deterministic-state sections. |
| **2** (comparison `meaningful-tradeoff`, `unknown-and-conflict`) | Supplier/quote ownership checks, `validate_analysis` lines 564–580. The former's `important_unknowns[1]` lists only C but cites A/B prices. In the latter, repair changes C's supplier ID from `3c89f597-ff08-49f3-bea8-579920457eea` to nonexistent `3c89f597-0ef8-49f3-bea8-579920457eea` in C-specific points, including `supplier_summaries[2]` and `important_unknowns[0]`. | Invalid model references; correct scope/provenance rejection. Copy exact IDs from context and retain all actual owners for cited fields. Do not infer or repair IDs in the validator. |

The prompt asks for grounded prose but does not expose the validator's closed state-assertion grammar. Its conflict guidance already forbids known fields as conflicts, but does not demonstrate that **every citation** in a point is a state assertion rather than background support. UNKNOWN guidance is less explicit about this distinction. Generic `AnalysisPoint` schema descriptions do not explain it. These are prompt/contract-guidance gaps. The supplied synthetic comparisons also combine A's 25-day known quote with a CONFLICT lead evaluation and B's 38-day quote with MEETS; that explains why outputs repeatedly discuss discrepancies. Preserve the supplied deterministic states and uncertainty rather than silently recomputing them; this observation does not justify reopening fixture/database work.

**Validator assessment:** confirmed false rejection of understandable, factually supported state-reporting prose under an intentionally closed grammar; no demonstrated incorrect deterministic state, provenance, ownership or section-membership check. Sentence-reset refusal is an existing ambiguity safeguard. The broad message conflates lexical residue with an unknown supplier/topic, making repair less actionable. This evidence justifies aligning generation/repair with the contract first, not broadening regex vocabulary, discarding residual text or relaxing owner/state checks. No accepted final or complete narrative-quality verdict follows from this diagnosis.

### Smallest justified correction proposal — not authorized or implemented

One shared, narrow **generation-contract guidance correction** could address the 19 grammar/binding cases and the overlapping section-citation mistakes across both entry points. It must cover both sentence construction and reference semantics; merely allowing `marked` or removing known citations from one example will not resolve the 20 failures. No exact live-success count is established.

Recommended bounded package:

1. In `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/tradeoff_analysis.py` and `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/comparison_advice.py`, amend only Analyst instructions/version to demonstrate one explicit supplier + one requirement-kind + one state per state sentence/point, e.g. `Supplier A certification is MEETS.` and `Supplier C certification is UNKNOWN.` Use actual supplied names/IDs/states, not hardcoded supplier roles. Repeat the subject after a sentence boundary; avoid attributive wrappers, pronouns and embedded UUIDs in state prose. Keep explanatory facts/provenance separate without dropping required failures, UNKNOWNs, conflicts or evidence. Explicitly require every UNKNOWN/CONFLICT section reference to match that state; a requirement-state point need not cite its related known quote value. Put currency/correction-verification caveats in summaries/tradeoffs. Copy IDs exactly. Preserve N4 lookup/budget guidance and null-suggestion rules.
2. In the existing `run_comparison_analyst` repair branch in `tradeoff_analysis.py`, retain the rejected final as prior assistant output in the repair request, alongside the existing validation feedback and original untrusted-data rules. This separately fixes the concrete omission affecting all 20 repairs. Do not echo it as authoritative instructions, add tools/retries, or change validation acceptance. Better field-path diagnostics may help later, but are not necessary to the smallest package and should not expand this into a validator/observability rewrite.
3. Regression changes only in `apps/api/tests/test_tradeoff_analysis.py` and `apps/api/tests/test_comparison_advice.py`; results in this document. Total proposed scope: **two production files, two existing test files, M6-U1.md**. No schema, domain, validator, fixture/scorer, provider configuration or dependency changes.

Required prospective regressions: exercise canonical state points with both entry points against existing snapshots (including real UNKNOWN/CONFLICT and required failure coverage); paired wrong-state/unknown-topic/unknown-owner/missing-citation cases must still reject. Cover a requirement CONFLICT with a known lead field: separate correctly cited points accept, grouped known-field conflict rejects. Cover known corrected price with provenance caution outside UNKNOWN versus the retained tie mistake. Cover explicit-name UNKNOWN after a numeric sentence while retaining rejection of unresolved pronouns/mixed owners. Cover the retained wrong supplier/quote UUID and cross-owner citation failures. Use existing fake-client seams to inspect the second request: it must contain the exact first rejected final, original snapshot/tool history and validation feedback; repaired valid output succeeds, second invalid final terminates with one repair and unchanged four-turn/six-tool limits. Run the existing binding/provenance/suggestion/budget regressions after separate implementation authorization. Do not rely only on instruction-substring assertions or canned fake outputs that ignore repair input.

Risks: canonical prose can reduce readability or encourage omitted qualifiers; keep all disclosures in separate cited points. More repair context adds tokens but must not alter limits or treat rejected assertions as truth. Prompt/version changes require fresh request provenance and cannot rebind historical results. Existing tests demonstrate deterministic acceptance/guard behavior, not provider compliance. A provider run would need separate authorization; none is proposed for this investigation. Even this package is not proven to eliminate all terminal failures or establish narrative groundedness.

### N4 attribution and disposition

**No evidence establishes an N4 regression.** Direct comparison with the retained pre-N4 `tradeoff_analysis.py.before` and `comparison_advice.py.before` in `apps/api/.eval-results/n4-validation-2026-11-03/` confirms all function/class ASTs, including validation and repair, are unchanged. The saved incremental diffs change instructions and prompt versions only. N4 did not introduce the closed parser or loss of rejected-final repair context. Its guidance could influence generated phrasing, but this single stochastic post-change run cannot isolate that causal effect. Historical cases often stopped at limits before reaching these checks; current lookup/limit failures do not recur. Historical configuration differences and an unchanged model alias do not supply a controlled comparison. Do not roll back N4 or call the increase in semantic errors proof of causation.

Prioritize terminal validity with the bounded package above; keep all legitimate rejection safeguards and official ERROR/exit-2 results. **M6-U1 stays IN_REVIEW; all ten human reviews remain PENDING with null review_result. Stop before implementation, Done Check, staging or commit.**


## Approved bounded Analyst semantic-repair correction — 2026-11-03

### Authorization and implemented scope

The user explicitly approved the focused terminal-failure proposal, limited to the two production files, two existing test files and this document, with no provider calls or live baseline. This is implementation and incremental self-inspection, **not independent Review or Done Check**. M6-U1 remains **IN_REVIEW**.

- `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/tradeoff_analysis.py`: additive canonical supplier-bound state and section-citation guidance; prompt `tradeoff-analysis-v2` → `tradeoff-analysis-v3`; one statement appends the exact rejected final as an assistant message to the existing run-local input history before the existing repair request.
- `apps/api/sourcepilot/ai/comparison_advice.py`: the same additive guidance; prompt `comparison-advice-v2` → `comparison-advice-v3`. Contract remains `comparison-advice-v1`.
- `apps/api/tests/test_tradeoff_analysis.py` and `apps/api/tests/test_comparison_advice.py`: 37 focused cases, plus updating only the expected current version in the two existing N4 prompt checks. All N4 guidance assertions and budget tests remain.
- This document: current status, results, limitations and provenance. Earlier records remain historical.

The prompts demonstrate one exact supplier name, requirement kind and state per sentence, with supplied identities/states replacing example roles. They discourage attribution wrappers, sentence-reset pronouns, embedded UUIDs and unsupported conjunctions. Numeric facts and correction/currency/source-verification cautions remain in separate cited summary/tradeoff points. Every UNKNOWN/CONFLICT section citation must match its deterministic state: a conflicting requirement does not make its related known quote field conflicting, and missing verification does not make a corrected known price missing. Genuine uncertainty, conflicts and required failures remain explicit. Exact IDs and actual citation owners must come from supplied context.

The rejected answer is retained only in the current invocation's local `input_items`, alongside the original snapshot and tool history; the existing validation error remains in repair instructions. Both prompts explicitly treat the rejected final and feedback as untrusted correction data, not instructions or evidence, and ask for a whole-answer recheck. There is no continuation identifier, shared cache, new retry or parsing layer. The normal second invalid final still terminates with VALIDATION_FAILED.

Incremental production AST comparison against saved starting files proves that, after excluding INSTRUCTIONS/PROMPT_VERSION and the single new assistant-message append, all production AST is identical. Semantic validators, schemas, tools/descriptions, dispatcher, supplier/session scope and freshness checks, counters, provider retry, one-repair behavior, four-model-turn/six-successful-tool defaults and workflow behavior are preserved. Existing N1–N4 corrections remain intact. No dependencies, datasets, expected outputs, scorer changes, historical report rewrites, new verification framework or future-unit work.

### Remaining blockers and classification limits

- **Ten execution-classified failures remain.** All have AIRun `VALIDATION_FAILED`, validation `INVALID`, exactly one REPAIR, and a schema-valid final rejected semantically. Read-only replay of retained responses against their persisted snapshots reproduces terminal unsupported-topic/supplier errors in nine cases and ambiguous supplier references in comparison `meaningful-tradeoff`. They are not provider transport failures, schema failures or turn/tool-limit exhaustion. The existing conservative classifier labels schema-valid semantic failures **RUNTIME_ERROR** rather than assuming model attribution; preserve that official classification and exit 2. The v3 correction reduced the count but did not eliminate terminal semantic-validation failure.
- **Nine QUALITY_FINDING outputs completed the runtime.** All nine trigger the relevant bounded narrative signal. Comparison `required-failure` additionally fails the scorer's `analysis_contract_states_references` assertion with unsupported topic/supplier, despite runtime REPAIRED and successful replay against its real snapshot. Suggestion `mixed-currencies` and `unequal-quantities` additionally fail `allowed_suggestion_choice`: both return Supplier A where the case's allowed choices reject that suggestion. These exact findings remain critical. The generic narrative signals require inspection/human judgment; they are not automatically proven hallucinations and are not execution errors.
- **Separate replay inconsistency remains unresolved.** Read-only validation of the captured final against the current persisted snapshot rejects comparison `corrected-quote-provenance` and suggestion `mixed-currencies` for ambiguous supplier references, although their original AIRuns recorded successful validation (REPAIRED and VALID respectively). The inspection is new diagnostic evidence, not a rerun or authoritative replacement for the runtime trace. No source hash changed. This task does not establish the cause or justify relabeling those cases; preserve both records and flag the discrepancy before claiming reproducible semantic acceptance. The scorer-versus-runtime difference in comparison `required-failure` is likewise visible, not silently accepted.
- All 20 model cases stayed within **2–3 model turns and 1–5 successful tools**. Four successful outputs used repair; six succeeded without repair; ten failed after repair, for **14 normal repairs total**. No terminal provider errors, tool-scope failures, limit failures, harness errors or scorer execution errors were reported. All 11 controls retain their expected rejections.

The one passing case is automated acceptance only. Narrative groundedness remains `NOT_ESTABLISHED_BY_AUTOMATED_SCORING`. No human quality verdict is inferred.

## Focused diagnosis of the ten remaining Analyst failures — 2026-11-03

**Disposition: BLOCKED; no additional behavioral correction is sufficiently justified by this evidence.** Investigation is limited to the ten RUNTIME_ERROR cases in [focused v3 evidence](evidence/M6-U1/analyst-v3-focused-live-2026-11-03-01/baseline/results.json), compared with those same cases in the [preceding full v2 baseline](evidence/M6-U1/final-full-live-2026-11-03-01/baseline/results.json). No cleared case was revalidated. Read retained initial/repaired outputs, traces, prompts, repair/client code and existing tests. Used only read-only database transactions for their persisted snapshots and local inspection of unchanged semantic validators; no eval runner, provider, test suite, new verification infrastructure or database write.

### Diagnostic correction and exact terminal errors

The prior focused `inspect_results.py` uses `Path.read_text()` without an encoding. This Windows process defaults to **cp1252**, but the retained report is UTF-8. In comparison `meaningful-tradeoff`, the genuine apostrophe U+2019 in `Supplier A’s` becomes U+00E2/U+20AC/U+2122; the supplier-token matcher consumes the spurious letter and raises an ambiguous-reference error. Explicit UTF-8 decoding removes that diagnostic artifact and reaches the actual rejected attribution sentence. The report bytes and runtime are not corrupted. **This supersedes the prior one-ambiguous/nine-unsupported terminal diagnosis: all ten correctly decoded repaired finals reject with exactly `Requirement-state assertion has an unsupported topic or supplier.`** The official RUNTIME_ERROR/VALIDATION_FAILED results and historical inspection files remain unchanged. No conclusion about the previously reported replay discrepancies in cleared cases is drawn here; they were not reopened.

Each v3 case has a tool-call turn, a first final rejected by validation, one repair, and a second/repaired final rejected terminally: **three turns, one REPAIR, schema-valid JSON, AIRun VALIDATION_FAILED/INVALID**. “Initial output” below means that first rejected final; there is no separate third answer before repair. Paths are within `analysis` for suggestion cases. `E` below is the exact unsupported-topic/supplier error quoted above; `A` is exactly `Requirement-state assertion has ambiguous references.`

| Case | First rejected v3 final / feedback | Repaired final: first failing path and exact rejected fragment (terminal E in every row) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| comparison `meaningful-tradeoff` | E at `key_tradeoffs[1]`: `The comparison evaluates Supplier B and Supplier C as MEETS…` | `key_tradeoffs[1]`: `The supplied comparison evaluates Supplier A production lead time as CONFLICT` |
| comparison `unknown-and-conflict` | E at `key_tradeoffs[3]`: `so the comparison does not establish whether Supplier C meets that required certification` | `key_tradeoffs[4]`: `The comparison does not establish whether Supplier C meets the required GRS certification` |
| comparison `incomplete-evidence` | A at `key_tradeoffs[1]`: numeric list fragment `30 days for Supplier C` before a semicolon and a conflict mention | `key_tradeoffs[6]`: `Supplier A's quoted value does not resolve the supplied deterministic conflict` |
| comparison `general-vs-project` | E at `key_tradeoffs[2]`: same reporting clause as repaired final | `key_tradeoffs[2]`: `the comparison reports a conflict for the preferred production lead-time requirement` |
| comparison `mixed-currencies` | E at `key_tradeoffs[0]`: `The supplied comparison marks Supplier A's required GRS certification as MEETS` | `important_unknowns[1]`: `Supplier C shipping cost is UNKNOWN because the comparison has no current value for that field` |
| suggestion `clear-suggestion` | E at `supplier_summaries[1]`: `the deterministic comparison classifies this preferred requirement as CONFLICT` | `important_unknowns[0]`: `Supplier C certification is UNKNOWN for the required GRS certification criterion` |
| suggestion `critical-unknown` | E at `key_tradeoffs[0]`: lead-time requirement `is unresolved as a conflict despite the quote recording 25 days` | `key_tradeoffs[0]`: `Supplier A's production lead-time requirement is unresolved as a conflict despite the quote recording 25 days` |
| suggestion `required-failure` | E at `key_tradeoffs[0]`: `Supplier A is the only supplier with a confirmed MEETS result for the required GRS certification` | `key_tradeoffs[2]`: `Supplier A's 25-day quote value does not resolve the deterministic conflict for its preferred lead-time requirement` |
| suggestion `incomplete-evidence` | E at `key_tradeoffs[1]`: `while Supplier B production lead time is MEETS` | `key_tradeoffs[0]`: `The supplied deterministic evaluation for Supplier A production lead time is CONFLICT` |
| suggestion `required-conflict` | E at `key_tradeoffs[1]`: `the supplied deterministic result for the preferred production lead time is unresolved as a conflict` | `supplier_summaries[1]`: `the result does not establish whether Supplier B meets that requirement` |

In the v2 baseline, nine of these terminal errors were E; suggestion `critical-unknown` ended with A on sentence-reset `Its required GRS certification is UNKNOWN`. Most v2 first failures were in supplier summaries with reporting wrappers; comparison `incomplete-evidence` initially used `It MEETS…`, and suggestion `required-conflict` initially referenced an unknown quote field. V3 now supplies canonical state summaries and separately cited UNKNOWN/CONFLICT points, but unsupported paraphrases persist elsewhere. Nine repaired JSON objects differ from their initial rejected objects; comparison `general-vs-project` repeats the entire parsed object unchanged. Repair often moves the first failure instead of removing all failures. These are comparisons of retained answers, not new case outcomes or proof that all later assertions would pass.

### Causes, grouped by behavior rather than assertion totals

All ten share one boundary: `_validate_state_assertions` scans state words throughout prose, including lowercase `conflict`/`meets`, then requires a closed grammatical vocabulary. Canonical summaries do not exempt later explanatory prose.

- **Six terminal reporting/explanation cases:** comparison meaningful-tradeoff, incomplete-evidence, general-vs-project; suggestion critical-unknown, required-failure, incomplete-evidence. Reporting wrappers and phrases such as `does not resolve … conflict` leave unconsumed words. This is persistent model noncompliance with the existing canonical/independent-sentence guidance, plus limited guidance about ordinary explanatory mentions. General-vs-project is especially clear: the model repeats the rejected object despite repair.
- **Two terminal uncertainty-about-a-state cases:** comparison unknown-and-conflict and suggestion required-conflict. `does not establish whether … meets` is an uncertainty statement, not an affirmative MEETS assertion, but it still triggers the closed parser. This is a language-coverage/guidance limitation; the product meaning is reasonable. It does not establish a new validator regression or justify ignoring arbitrary negation tails. Canonical UNKNOWN/CONFLICT disclosures already carry the necessary uncertainty.
- **Two terminal qualification cases:** comparison mixed-currencies and suggestion clear-suggestion. `because …` and the additional noun `criterion` fall outside the accepted scaffolding despite correct underlying UNKNOWN facts. Both cases repair earlier reporting clauses but retain or introduce these extra forms. Guidance gives examples but no exhaustive grammar; incomplete compliance and undisclosed lexical restrictions overlap.

**Genuine defects versus limitations:** the cp1252 evidence reader is a confirmed diagnostic defect, not a production semantic-validation defect. No incorrect deterministic state, ownership check, section-membership check or accepted-grammar rejection is demonstrated by these ten correctly decoded terminal fragments. Rejection of sensible but unsupported natural language is a real usability limitation of the intentionally closed grammar (existing tests explicitly retain wrapper rejection), not evidence that a particular regex relaxation is safe. First-failure validation can mask further problems: comparison incomplete-evidence also retains `UNKNOWN because…`; suggestion supporting reasons/uncertainties contain further prose. The initial suggestion required-conflict also describes CONFLICT as failure; its repaired answer removes that conflation. No exhaustive content-quality PASS follows from this diagnosis.

### Did the approved repair-context correction operate?

**Supported as operating correctly at the application boundary; provider receipt/attention is not independently captured.** Consumed hashes for `tradeoff_analysis.py`, `comparison_advice.py` and `client.py` still match the focused run. The shared branch at `tradeoff_analysis.py` appends the exact first rejected `invocation.output_text` as an assistant message, retains the original snapshot and tool history, and adds the first validation error to the unchanged base instructions before turn three. `client.py` passes that list as Responses `input`. All ten traces show exactly this one-repair route and no intervening lookup or provider-error branch. The initial feedback is E for nine cases and A for comparison incomplete-evidence, as listed above.

The reviewed `SemanticRepairModel` / `test_semantic_repair_context_is_run_local_and_bounded` in `tests/test_comparison_advice.py` checks exact rejected text, original history, error feedback, isolation and termination for both entry points. Those tests were inspected, not rerun. CapturingClient retains responses/tool requests, not the outgoing repair body; the input hash binds the initial request rather than the full repair conversation. Thus repair context can be reconstructed from code and retained evidence, but is not a wire-level capture. There is no evidence of the old rejected-final omission recurring, nor evidence that the model used every supplied instruction. Generic feedback still lacks a field path or offending fragment.

### Bounded-correction decision and acceptance

**No new implementation package is proposed; proposed production/test files: none.** A third prompt refinement repeating “canonical sentences everywhere,” enumerating `criterion`/`because`/`whether`, or merely adding error locations could help some outputs, but these ten do not establish that any one such change addresses the remaining failure mechanisms. The current prompt already forbids wrappers and asks for a whole-answer recheck. Adding accepted vocabulary or suppressing state words inside arbitrary explanations would alter the deliberately closed validation boundary and needs broader design/negative coverage, not a small repair. Deterministic rendering or contract redesign is outside this investigation. No focused tests are commissioned without a justified implementation proposal.

Recommend a **documented human acceptance decision** about this known prose/grammar limitation rather than another tuning/evaluation cycle: retain BLOCKED under the current live-baseline requirement, or separately approve a scoped design change/explicit limitation. A manual judgment that particular prose is reasonable must not relabel these failed AIRuns, waive the full-baseline requirement silently, or fill the ten unrelated human-review verdicts. M6-U1 remains **IN_REVIEW**, ten human reviews **PENDING/null**. Only this document was edited; historical reports, code, prompts, validators, contracts, budgets, datasets and scoring remain unchanged. Stop before implementation, Done Check or commit.
